Low Regularity Well-Posedness of Cauchy Problem for Two-Dimensional Relativistic Euler Equation
Huali Zhang

Abstract
. In this article, we initiate the study of the Cauchy problem for the two-dimensional relativistic Euler equations in a low-regularity setting. By introducing good variables–a rescaled velocity, logarithmic enthalpy, and an appropriately defined vorticity, we reformulate the equations into a coupled wave-transport system. The nice structures, combined with new elliptic estimates and the Smith-Tataru approach [ 61 ] , enable us to extend ideas from the quasilinear wave equations to the two-dimensional relativistic Euler equations. First, we prove the existence and uniqueness of solutions when the initial logarithmic enthalpy h 0 h_{0} , rescaled velocity 𝐯 0 \mathbf{v}_{0} , and vorticity 𝐰 0 \mathbf{w}_{0} satisfy ( h 0 , 𝐯 0 , 𝐰 0 , ∇ 𝐰 0 ) ∈ H 7 4 + ​ ( ℝ 2 ) × H 7 4 + ​ ( ℝ 2 ) × H 3 2 + ​ ( ℝ 2 ) × L 8 ​ ( ℝ 2 ) (h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0},\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0})\in H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{3}{2}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{2}) . If the initial vorticity 𝐰 0 ∈ H 3 2 ​ ( ℝ 2 ) \mathbf{w}_{0}\in H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}) , then a strong Strichartz estimate holds on a time interval that depends on the frequency size. Inspired by the semiclassical analysis approach of Bahouri-Chemin [ 9 ] and Ai-Ifrim-Tataru [ 2 ] , we further derive a loss of derivatives in the Strichartz estimate when summing over the number of these intervals. This yields a relaxed well-posedness result when ( h 0 , 𝐯 0 , 𝐰 0 , ∇ 𝐰 0 ) ∈ H 7 4 + ​ ( ℝ 2 ) × H 7 4 + ​ ( ℝ 2 ) × H 3 2 ​ ( ℝ 2 ) × L 8 ​ ( ℝ 2 ) (h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0},\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0})\in H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{2}) . Both results are valid for the general state function p ⁡ ( ϱ ) = ϱ A p(\varrho)=\varrho^{A} ( A ≥ 1 A\geq 1 ). Secondly, in the special case where p ⁡ ( ϱ ) = ϱ p(\varrho)=\varrho , the acoustic metric reduces to the standard flat Minkowski metric. By applying Strichartz estimates to the wave part and combining with new elliptic estimate, we establish the well-posedness of solutions when ( h 0 , 𝐯 0 , 𝐰 0 ) ∈ H 7 4 + ​ ( ℝ 2 ) × H 7 4 + ​ ( ℝ 2 ) × H 1 + ​ ( ℝ 2 ) (h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})\in H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{1+}(\mathbb{R}^{2}) . The regularity exponents for the log-enthalpy and rescaled velocity correspond to those in Smith and Tataru [ 61 ] , while the vorticity regularity corresponds to Bourgain and Li [ 12 ] . Moreover, if the stiff flow is irrotational, we can prove the local well-posedness for ( h 0 , 𝐯 0 ) ∈ H 1 + ​ ( ℝ 2 ) (h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\in H^{1+}(\mathbb{R}^{2}) , and global well-posedness for small initial data ( h 0 , 𝐯 0 ) ∈ B ˙ 2 , 1 1 ​ ( ℝ 2 ) (h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\in\dot{B}^{1}_{2,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}) .



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 35Q35,35R05

1.
Introduction

1.1.
Background

In fluid mechanics and astrophysics, the relativistic Euler equations are a generalization of the Euler equations that account for the effects of general relativity. This paper addresses the Cauchy problem for the two-dimensional relativistic Euler equations with low regularity data. To formulate the system, we use the notations
\varrho
and
\mathbf{u}=(u^{0},u^{1},u^{2})^{\textrm{T}}
to represent the energy density and the relativistic velocity, respectively. Moreover, the velocity is assumed to be a forward time-like vector field, normalized by

u^{\alpha}u_{\alpha}=-1,

(1.1)

where
u_{\alpha}=m_{\alpha\beta}u^{\beta}
, and
\mathbf{m}=(m_{\alpha\beta})_{3\times 3}
is the flat standard Minkowski metric. Also, we use the Greek indices
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\cdots
take on the values
0,1,2
, while the Latin spatial indices
a,b,c,\cdots,i,j,k,\cdots
take on the values
1,2
. Repeated indices are summed over (from
0
to
2
if they are Greek, and from
1
to
2
if they are Latin). Greek and Latin indices are lowered and raised with the Minkowski metric
\mathbf{m}
and its inverse
\mathbf{m}^{-1}
. We will use this convention throughout the paper unless otherwise indicated.

Let
\mathcal{Q}
be the stress energy tensor, which is defined by

\mathcal{Q}^{\alpha\beta}=(p+\varrho)u^{\alpha}u^{\beta}+pm^{\alpha\beta}.

Then the motion of the relativistic Euler equations can be described by

\partial_{\alpha}\mathcal{Q}^{\alpha\beta}=0.

Projecting the above equation into the subspace
parallel and orthogonal to
u^{\alpha}
yields (c.f. Disconzi-Ifrim-Tataru
[
17
]
or Oliynyk
[
55
,
56
]
)

\begin{cases}u^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\varrho+(p+\varrho)\partial_{\kappa}u^{\kappa}=0,\qquad t>0,\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{2},\\
(p+\varrho)u^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}u^{\alpha}+(m^{\alpha\kappa}+u^{\alpha}u^{\kappa})\partial_{\kappa}p=0.\end{cases}

(1.2)

An appropriate equation of state (please c.f. Choquet-Bruhat
[
13
]
) is of the form

p=p(\varrho)=\varrho^{A}.

(1.3)

where
A
is the adiabatic constant. If
A>1
, the fluid is called polytropic. When
A=1
, it is referred to as a stiff fluid, in which the speed of sound equals the speed of light. Both in mathematics and physics, the system (
1.2
)-(
1.3
) has attracted significant attention. In our paper, we aim to study low-regularity solutions to the Cauchy problem for the two-dimensional relativistic Euler equations. Next, let us review some historical results related to the compressible or relativistic Euler equations.

1.2.
Historical results

The local well-posedness theory for the compressible Euler equations began with Majda’s work
[
48
]
, where the initial velocity, density, and entropy belong to the Sobolev space
H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
with
s>1+\frac{n}{2}
. In the special case of isentropic and irrotational, the compressible Euler equations can be written as a quasilinear wave system. A general quasilinear wave equation takes the form

\begin{cases}&\square_{h(\phi)}\phi=q(d\phi,d\phi),\qquad t>0,x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\\
&(\phi,\partial_{t}\phi)|_{t=0}=(\phi_{0},\phi_{1})\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\times H^{s-1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\end{cases}

(1.4)

where
\phi
is a scalar function,
h(\phi)
is a Lorentzian metric depending on
\phi
,
d=(\partial_{t},\partial_{1},\partial_{2},\cdots,\partial_{n})
, and
q
is quadratic in
d\phi
. The sharp local well-posedness was obtained by Smith and Tataru
[
61
]
for
s>\frac{7}{4}(n=2)
and
s>2(n=3)
( c.f. Lindblad
[
46
]
and Ohlman
[
54
]
for ill-posedness). We also highlight significant related progress
[
9
,
10
,
26
,
29
,
30
,
35
,
52
,
58
,
59
,
60
,
62
,
63
,
64
,
66
]
on the low regularity problems involving quasilinear wave equations. There are also two other important quasilinear wave models, namely, Einstein vacuum and time-like minimal surface equations. Regarding Einstein vacuum equations, the results depend on the choice of gauge. In the Yang-Mills gauge, Klainerman-Rodnianski-Szeftel
[
37
]
solved the well-known
L^{2}
curvature conjecture. In wave gauge, the problem is well-posed in
H^{2+}
and ill-posed in
H^{2}
, cf. Klainerman-Rodnianski
[
36
]
and Ettinger-Lindblad
[
20
]
. In the CMC gauge, the system is well-posed in
H^{2+}
, please refer Andersson-Moncrief
[
6
]
and Wang
[
65
]
. For the time-like minimal surface equations, which satisfy null conditions, a celebrated work was obtained by Ai-Ifrim-Tataru
[
2
]
, namely by lowering
\frac{1}{8}
derivatives in two space dimensions and by
\frac{1}{4}
derivatives in higher dimensions. For radial initial data, Wang and Zhou
[
69
]
proved the critical global well-posedness in two dimensions. Very recently, By finding a good gauge and applying a parametric representation, Moschidis and Rodnianski
[
53
]
reduced the additional
\frac{1}{12}
-order regularity requirement for time-like surface equations in three dimensions.

In the general case, the compressible Euler equations can be written as a coupled wave-transport system (c.f. Luk-Speck
[
47
]
). Based on this formulation, Disconzi-Luo-Mazzone-Speck
[
18
]
proved the existence and uniqueness of solutions of compressible Euler equations when the initial velocity
\mathbf{u}_{0}
, density
\rho_{0}
, vorticity
\mathbf{w}_{0}
, and entropy
S_{0}
satisfy
(\mathbf{u}_{0},\rho_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0},S_{0})\in H^{2+}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{2+}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{2+}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{3+}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
with Hölder condition
\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{w}_{0},\Delta S_{0}\in C^{\sigma},0<\sigma<1
. Meanwhile, Wang
[
67
]
established the existence and uniqueness of solutions of 3D isentropic compressible Euler equations if
(\mathbf{u}_{0},\rho_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{s_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, where
2<s_{0}<s
. The work
[
18
]
and
[
67
]
both are based on vector-fields approach. By applying Smith-Tataru’s method
[
61
]
, together with semi-classical analysis and Tao’s frequency envelope, Andersson and Zhang
[
7
]
proved a complete local well-posedness of 3D compressible Euler equations with relaxed assumptions
(\mathbf{u}_{0},\rho_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})\in H^{2+}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{2+}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
or
(\mathbf{u}_{0},\rho_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0},S_{0})\in H^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{\frac{3}{2}+}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{\frac{5}{2}+}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. In two dimensions, Zhang
[
76
,
77
,
79
]
recently proved the well-posedness of solutions of isentropic compressible Euler equations when
(\mathbf{u}_{0},\rho_{0},w_{0},\nabla w_{0})\in H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. In the reverse direction, the ill-posedness results were established by An-Chen-Yin
[
4
,
5
]
if
(\mathbf{u}_{0},\rho_{0})\in\dot{H}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
or
(\mathbf{u}_{0},\rho_{0})\in\dot{H}^{\frac{7}{4}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
with smooth vorticity.

Let us return to the study of the relativistic Euler equations. The early work on the Cauchy problem is attributed to Bruhat
[
22
]
(see also Friedrich
[
21
]
). Makino-Ukai
[
49
,
50
]
established the local well-posedness of the Cauchy problem for the three-dimensional relativistic Euler equations with initial data in
H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
for
s\geq 3
. For an overview of the standard theory, we refer to the relevant chapter in Choquet-Bruhat’s book
[
13
]
. With vacuum, the pioneering result was obtained by Rendall
[
57
]
and Guo-Tahvildar-Zadeh
[
24
]
. By introducing a new symmetrization, LeFloch-Ukai
[
43
]
established a local-in-time existence result for solutions in
n
spatial dimensions when the initial data belong to
H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
with
s>1+\frac{n}{2}
. Meanwhile, there are several interesting results on free boundary problems by Avadanei
[
8
]
, Jang-LeFloch-Masmoudi
[
28
]
, Had
\check{\mathrm{z}}
i
\acute{\mathrm{c}}
-Shkoller-Speck
[
25
]
, Disconzi-Ifrim-Tataru
[
17
]
, and Miao-Shahshahani
[
51
]
and so on. Recently, Disconzi and Speck
[
19
]
proposed a new formulation of wave-transport equations for the three-dimensional relativistic Euler equations, in which the inherent structure plays a crucial role in addressing low-regularity problems. Based on
[
18
]
and
[
19
]
, Yu
[
74
]
proved the existence and uniqueness of rough solutions of 3D relativistic Euler equations when the initial enthalpy
h_{0}
, velocity
\mathbf{u}_{0}
, vorticity
\mathbf{w}_{0}
, and entropy
S_{0}
satisfy
(h_{0},\mathbf{u}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0},S_{0})\in H^{2+}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{2+}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{2+}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{3+}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, and
\mathrm{vort}\mathbf{w}_{0},\Delta S_{0}\in C^{\sigma}(0<\sigma<1)
. Very recently, Zhang
[
78
]
proved the complete local well-posedness of the 3D relativistic Euler equations with initial data
(h_{0},\mathbf{u}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})\in H^{2+}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{2+}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. This result also resolves the “open problem D” proposed by Disconzi
[
16
]
.

1.3.
Motivation

Although there are several low regularity results
[
19
,
74
,
78
]
for the 3D relativistic Euler equations, the Cauchy problem for the 2D case remains unknown. In three dimensions, the analysis crucially relies on the
\mathbf{u}
-vorticity formulation, which effectively arises in the wave component of velocity and has nice transport dynamics. However, this approach does not directly generalize to two dimensions, as it may not yield effective transport structures. On the other hand, low regularity well-posedness relies on Strichartz estimates, which differ between two and three dimensions. Strongly motivated by these considerations, we study low regularity solutions to the Cauchy problem for the 2D relativistic Euler equations.

To overcome the associated difficulties, we draw inspiration from
[
17
]
and
[
19
]
, where a rescaled velocity and logarithmic enthalpy are introduced, respectively. Combining their idea, we define new variables, including rescaled velocity and logarithmic enthalpy, which reveal a coupled wave-transport structure and allow for a proper definition of vorticity in the 2D relativistic Euler equations. Under this reformulation, we apply the method of Smith and Tataru
[
61
]
(originally introduced for quasilinear wave equations) to establish the first low regularity result for the Cauchy problem of 2D relativistic Euler equations. Our main results(see section
2
below) can be summarized as follows:

•

in the general polytropic case, i.e.
p(\varrho)=\varrho^{A}
with the constant
A\geq 1
, the acoustic metric depends on log-enthalpy
h
and rescaled velocity
\mathbf{v}
, making the system (
1.11
) genuinely quasilinear. Using the energy method, the key step is to bound the Strichartz estimate
\|dh,d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}
. Following
[
61
]
, we derive Strichartz estimates for linear wave equations equipped with the acoustic metric by using wave packets along characteristic hypersurfaces. By carefully analyzing the characteristic hypersurfaces, a regularity exponent of
\frac{3}{2}+
is sufficient. Indeed, the vorticity and acoustic metric determines the geometry of characteristic hypersurfaces. Consequently, the existence and uniqueness of (
1.11
) holds if the initial data
(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0},\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0})
belong to the spaces
H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{3}{2}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. By applying the Strichartz estimates again and combining them with semiclassical analysis, the existence and uniqueness of (
1.11
) also holds if the initial data
(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0},\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0})\in H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. This result can be seen as an extension of the author’s result
[
79
]
from the Newtonian compressible Euler equations to the relativistic case.

•

in the stiff case, i.e.
p(\varrho)=\varrho
, the acoustic metric is a flat Minkowski metric. Applying the Strichartz estimates in
[
32
]
, new elliptic estimate and bootstrap arguments, we can prove the local well-posedness of (
1.11
) if the initial data
(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})\in H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{1+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. The regularity exponents for the log-enthalpy and rescaled velocity correspond to those in
[
61
]
, while the vorticity regularity corresponds to
[
12
]
.

•

in the stiff case, for irrotational flow, the system becomes a semi-linear wave equations satisfying “wave map” null conditions. Therefore, referring to the classical results in
[
38
]
and
[
72
]
, the local well-posedness of (
1.11
) holds if
(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\in H^{1+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and the small, global well-posedness holds if
(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\in\dot{B}_{2,1}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.

We next define several good variables as follows.

1.4.
The good variables

Let us introduce some variables–acoustic speed, number density, enthalpy, and vorticity.

Definition 1.1
.

[
19
]

We denote the acoustic speed
c_{s}

c_{s}=\sqrt{\frac{dp}{d\varrho}},

(1.5)

the number density
q

p+\varrho=q\frac{d\varrho}{dq},

(1.6)

and the enthalpy per particle
H

H=\frac{p+\varrho}{q}.

(1.7)

Due to the work of Disconiz, Ifrim, and Tataru (
[
17
]
, page 132, equation (1.9)), the rescaled velocity exhibits a better structure than the original velocity. Following this idea, we introduce the rescaled velocity as follows.

Definition 1.2
.

We denote the logarithmic enthalpy
h

h=\log H,

(1.8)

and the rescaled velocity

\mathbf{v}=\mathrm{e}^{h}\mathbf{u}.

(1.9)

Remark 1.1
.

(1)

To ensure that the system (
1.2
) is hyperbolic, we assume
0<c_{s}\leq 1
throughout the paper.

(2)

Due to (
1.1
) and (
1.9
), we have

\mathrm{e}^{-2h}v^{\alpha}v_{\alpha}=-1.

(1.10)

If we set
\mathbf{v}=(v^{0},\mathring{\mathbf{v}})
, then

v^{0}=\sqrt{\mathrm{e}^{2h}+|\mathring{\mathbf{v}}|^{2}}.

Under the settings (
1.5
), (
1.6
), (
1.7
), and (
1.8
), then
\varrho,p
, and
c_{s}
are functions of
h
. To be simple, we record

\varrho=\varrho(h),\quad p=p(h),\quad c_{s}=c_{s}(h).

In terms of these variables, the relativistic Euler equations (
1.2
) becomes (see Lemma
3.2
below)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}&(1-c^{2}_{s})v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}h+c^{2}_{s}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\kappa}=0,\\
&v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\alpha}+\mathrm{e}^{2h}\partial^{\alpha}h=0.\end{cases}

(1.11)

We will work with this system for the rest of the paper. To study the low regularity solutions, we need a precise wave-transport structure for (
1.11
). Therefore, we define the metric
g
and the vorticity
\mathbf{w}
as follows.

Definition 1.3
.

Define the function
\Theta=\Theta(h,\mathbf{v})
as

\Theta=\frac{1}{c^{2}_{s}-\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(c^{2}_{s}-1)(v^{0})^{2}}.

(1.12)

Define the acoustic metric
g=(g^{\alpha\beta})_{3\times 3}
by

\begin{split}g^{\alpha\beta}=&\Theta\left\{c^{2}_{s}m^{\alpha\beta}+\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\alpha}v^{\beta}\right\}.\end{split}

(1.13)

Define the vorticity
\mathbf{w}=(w^{0},w^{1},w^{2})
as

w^{\alpha}=\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}v_{\gamma}.

(1.14)

and
\mathbf{W}=(W^{0},W^{1},W^{2})
as

W^{\alpha}=\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}+(1-c^{-2}_{s})\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}h.

(1.15)

Remark 1.2
.

If
p(\varrho)=\varrho
, then
c_{s}\equiv 1
. In this case, using (
1.12
) and (
1.13
), the acoustic metric
g
is a flat Minkowski metric and

g=\mathbf{m}.

Inspired by Wang’s paper
[
67
]
, we also introduce a decomposition of rescaled velocity.

Definition 1.4
.

Let
h
and
\mathbf{v}
be the logarithmic enthalpy and rescaled velocity. Let
\mathbf{w}
and
\mathbf{W}
be defined in (
1.14
)- (
1.15
). Let
\mathbf{Id}
be the identity operator. Define the vector
\mathbf{v}_{-}=(v_{-}^{0},v_{-}^{1},v_{-}^{2})^{\mathrm{T}}
by

\begin{split}(\mathbf{Id}-\mathbf{P})v_{-}^{\alpha}&=\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma},\end{split}

(1.16)

where

\mathbf{P}=(m^{\beta\gamma}+2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}v^{\beta}v^{\gamma})\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}.

(1.17)

Meanwhile, we define the vector
\mathbf{v}_{+}=(v_{+}^{0},v_{+}^{1},v_{+}^{2})^{\mathrm{T}}
by

v^{\alpha}_{+}=v^{\alpha}-v^{\alpha}_{-}.

(1.18)

Remark 1.3
.

From (
1.17
), we can verify that
\mathbf{{P}}
is a space-time elliptic operator throughout the entire space-time domain; for details, see Lemma
3.11
below. From (
1.17
), we can verify that
\mathbf{{P}}
is a space-time elliptic operator throughout the entire space-time domain; for details, see Lemma
3.11
below.

Therefore, the operator
\mathbf{P}
differs from the spatial elliptic operator defined in
[
67
]
.

1.5.
Wave-transport reduction

Lemma 1.1
.

Let
(h,\mathbf{v})
be a solution of (
1.11
). Let
g
and
\mathbf{w}
be defined in (
1.13
) and (
1.14
). Then the system (
1.11
) can be written as

\begin{cases}&\square_{g}h=\mathcal{D},\\
&\square_{g}v^{\alpha}=-c^{2}_{s}\Theta\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}+Q^{\alpha},\\
&v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w^{\alpha}=w^{\kappa}\partial^{\alpha}v_{\kappa}-w^{\alpha}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\kappa},\end{cases}

(1.19)

where
\square_{g}=g^{\alpha\beta}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}
,
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathbf{Q}=(Q^{0},Q^{1},Q^{2})
are quadratic terms

\begin{split}\mathcal{D}=&-2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}\Theta c^{-1}_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}v^{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\beta}h\partial_{\kappa}h-\mathrm{e}^{-2h}\Theta c^{2}_{s}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}-\Theta(1+c^{2}_{s})\partial_{\kappa}h\partial^{\kappa}h,\end{split}

(1.20)

and

\begin{split}Q^{\alpha}=&-\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(c^{2}_{s}-1)\Theta v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\alpha}-2(c^{2}_{s}-1)\Theta v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}h\partial^{\alpha}h-2\Theta c_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}\partial^{\alpha}h\partial_{\kappa}v^{\kappa}\\
&+(c^{2}_{s}-1)\Theta\partial^{\alpha}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}h+2\Theta c_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}v^{\beta}\partial^{\alpha}h\partial_{\beta}h.\end{split}

(1.21)

Remark 1.4
.

For the proof of Lemma
1.1
, please refer to Lemma
3.3
and
3.5
below.

1.6.
Notations

•

Define the operators
\nabla=(\partial_{1},\partial_{2})^{\mathrm{T}}
,
d=(\partial_{t},\partial_{1},\partial_{2})^{\mathrm{T}}
,
\Delta=\partial^{2}_{1}+\partial^{2}_{2}
,
\Lambda_{x}=(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}
,
\square=-\partial^{2}_{tt}+\Delta
,
\square_{g}=g^{\alpha\beta}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}
, and

\mathbf{T}=(v^{0})^{-1}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}=\partial_{t}+(v^{0})^{-1}v^{i}\partial_{i}.

(1.22)

•

Let
\left<\xi\right>=(1+|\xi|^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}},\ \xi\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Denote by
\left<\nabla\right>
the corresponding Bessel potential multiplier.

•

Let
\zeta
be a smooth function with support in the shell
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\frac{1}{2}\leq|\xi|\leq 2\}
. Here,
\xi
denotes the variable of the spatial Fourier transform. Let
\zeta
also satisfy the
condition
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\zeta(2^{k}\xi)=1
. Let
{P}_{j}
be the Littlewood-Paley operator with frequency
2^{j},j\in\mathbb{Z}
(cf. Bahouri-Chemin-Danchin
[
11
]
, page 78),

{P}_{j}f=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}x\cdot\xi}\zeta(2^{-j}\xi)\hat{f}(\xi)d\xi.

For
f\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, let

\displaystyle\|f\|_{H^{s}}=\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

with the homogeneous norm
\|f\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s}}={\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}}2^{2js}\|{P}_{j}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
.
To avoid confusion, when the function
f
is related to both time and space variables, we use the notation
\|f\|_{H_{x}^{s}}=\|f(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
.

•

Specially,
\mathbf{v}\in H^{s}_{x}
means
\mathbf{v}-(1,0,0)^{\mathrm{T}}\in H^{s}_{x}
.

•

For
a\in\mathbb{R}
, we define

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{a}_{\infty,2}}=\left(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{2ja}\|P_{j}f\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

•

Assume there exists two positive constants
C_{0}
and
c_{0}
such that

|h_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0}|\leq C_{0},\qquad 0<c_{0}<c_{s}|_{t=0}<1,

(1.23)

where
{\mathbf{v}}_{0}=(v^{0}_{0},v^{1}_{0},v^{2}_{0})
.

•

We use three small parameters

0<\epsilon_{3}\ll\epsilon_{2}\ll\epsilon_{1}\ll\epsilon_{0}\ll 1.

(1.24)

•

In the following, constants
C
depending only on
C_{0},c_{0}
are called universal. Unless otherwise stated, all constants that appear are universal in this sense.
The notation
X\lesssim Y
means
X\leq CY
, where
C
is a universal constant, possibly depending on
C_{0},c_{0}
. Similarly, we write
X\simeq Y
when
C_{1}Y\leq X\leq C_{2}Y
, with
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
universal constants, and
X\ll Y
when
X\leq CY
for a sufficiently large constant
C
. The universal constant may change from line to line.

2.
Main results

We present four theorems concerning the well-posedness of Cauchy problem (
1.19
).

2.1.
Statement of results.

Our first result is stated as follows.

Theorem 2.1
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq\frac{15}{8}
. Consider the Cauchy problem (
1.19
). Let
\mathbf{w}
be defined in (
1.14
). For any given initial data
(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
satisfies (
1.23
), and for any
M_{0}>0
such that

\|(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{L^{8}}\leq M_{0},

(2.1)

there exist positive constants
T>0
and
M_{1}>0
(
T
and
{M}_{1}
depends on
C_{0},c_{0},s,s_{0},{M}_{0}
)
such that (
1.19
) has a unique solution
(h,\mathbf{v})\in C([0,T],H_{x}^{s})\cap C^{1}([0,T],H_{x}^{s-1})
,

\mathbf{w}\in C([0,T],H_{x}^{s_{0}})\cap C^{1}([0,T],H_{x}^{s_{0}-1})
. Moreover,

(1)

the solution
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
satisfy the energy estimate

\begin{split}&\|(h,\mathbf{v})\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}L_{x}^{8}}\leq M_{1},\end{split}

(2.2)

and

\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,{T}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq 1+C_{0}.

(2)

the solution also satisfies the Strichartz estimate

\|dh,d\mathbf{v}_{+},d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{4}_{[0,T]}L_{x}^{\infty}}\leq M_{1},

(2.3)

where
\mathbf{v}_{+}
is denoted in (
1.18
).

(3)

for any
1\leq r\leq s+1
, and for each
t_{0}\in[0,T)
, the Cauchy problem of the linear equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{g}f=0,\qquad(t,x)\in(t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2},\\
&(f,\partial_{t}f)|_{t=t_{0}}=(f_{0},f_{1})\in H_{x}^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H_{x}^{r-1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),\end{cases}

(2.4)

admits a solution
f\in C([0,T],H_{x}^{r})\times C^{1}([0,T],H_{x}^{r-1})
. The following estimate holds:

\|f\|_{L_{[0,T]}^{\infty}H_{x}^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}f\|_{L_{[0,T]}^{\infty}H_{x}^{r-1}}\leq C_{M_{0}}(\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}).

Additionally, the following estimates hold, provided
k<r-\frac{3}{4}
,

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{4}_{[0,T]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C_{M_{0}}(\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}),

(2.5)

and the same estimates hold with
\left<\nabla\right>^{k}
replaced by
\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d
. Here,
C_{M_{0}}
is a constant depending on
C_{0},c_{0},s,s_{0}
and
M_{0}
.

Remark 2.1
.

(1)

The initial condition
(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\in H^{\frac{7}{4}+}
ensures the Strichartz admissibility for 2D wave system. The vorticity
\mathbf{w}_{0}\in H^{\frac{3}{2}+}
controls the geometry condition of null hypersurface in section
6
. Meanwhile, the additional condition
\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0}\in L^{8}
is imposed specifically to close the energy estimates for vorticity due to commutator estimate.

(2)

Theorem
2.1
is valid for the general state
p(\varrho)=\varrho^{A}
(
A\geq 1
).

Our second result is stated below.

Theorem 2.2
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s\leq\frac{15}{8}
. Consider the Cauchy problem (
1.19
). Let
\mathbf{w}
be defined in (
1.14
). For any given initial data
(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
satisfies (
1.23
), and for any
M_{0}>0
such that

\|(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{L^{8}}\leq M_{0},

(2.6)

there exist positive constants
T^{*}>0
and
M_{2}>0
(
T^{*}
and
{M}_{2}
depends on
C_{0},c_{0},s,{M}_{0}
) such that (
1.19
)
has a unique solution
(h,\mathbf{v})\in C([0,T^{*}],H_{x}^{s})\cap C^{1}([0,T^{*}],H_{x}^{s-1})
,

\mathbf{w}\in C([0,T^{*}],H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}})\cap C^{1}([0,T^{*}],H_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}})
. Moreover,
the following statements hold:

(1)

The solutions
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
satisfy the energy estimate

\begin{split}&\|(h,\mathbf{v})\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T^{*}]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T^{*}]}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T^{*}]}L_{x}^{8}}\leq M_{2},\end{split}

and

\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,{T^{*}}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq 1+C_{0}.

(2)

The solution also satisfies the Strichartz estimate

\|dh,d\mathbf{v}_{+},d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{4}_{[0,T^{*}]}L_{x}^{\infty}}\leq M_{2},

where
\mathbf{v}_{+}
is denoted in (
1.18
).

(3)

For any
s-\frac{3}{4}\leq r\leq\frac{11}{4}
, and for each
t_{0}\in[0,T)
, the Cauchy problem of the linear equation
(
2.4
) admits a solution
f\in C([0,T^{*}],H_{x}^{r})\times C^{1}([0,T^{*}],H_{x}^{r-1})
. The following estimate holds:

\|f\|_{L_{[0,T^{*}]}^{\infty}H_{x}^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}f\|_{L_{[0,T^{*}]}^{\infty}H_{x}^{r-1}}\leq{M_{3}}(\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}).

Additionally, the following estimates hold, provided
k<r-(s-1)
,

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{4}_{[0,T^{*}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq{M_{3}}(\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}),

and the same estimates hold with
\left<\nabla\right>^{k}
replaced by
\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d
. Here,
{M_{3}}
is a positive constant depending on
C_{0},c_{0},s
and
M_{0}
.

Remark 2.2
.

(1)

Compared with Theorem
2.1
, Theorem
2.2
lowers the regularity exponent of the vorticity from
\frac{3}{2}+
to exactly
\frac{3}{2}
. This reduction is non-trivial because, if we apply Smith-Tataru’s approach directly, we cannot obtain the Strichartz estimates for velocity and density when
w_{0}\in H^{\frac{3}{2}}
.

When
(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0},\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0})\in H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we first use the frequency truncation to get a sequence of initial data belonging in
H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{3}{2}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. Based on Theorem
2.1
, a sequence of solutions on a short time-interval, and these intervals depends on the size of frequency.
By extending the solutions from these short time intervals to a uniformly regular time-interval, a Strichartz estimate with a loss of derivatives can be obtained. This leads us to prove the local existence and uniqueness of solutions to the 2D compressible Euler equations for initial data
(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0},\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0})\in H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{7}{4}+}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times L^{8}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.

(2)

Theorem
2.2
is also valid for the general state
p(\varrho)=\varrho^{A}
(
A\geq 1
).

Very interestingly, in the special case where
p(\varrho)=\varrho
, namely the stiff fluid, the system (
1.19
) becomes (see Lemma
10.1
below)

\begin{cases}&\square h=\mathrm{e}^{-2h}w^{\beta}w_{\beta}-2\partial_{\beta}h\partial^{\beta}h-\mathrm{e}^{-2h}\partial_{\beta}v^{\alpha}\partial^{\beta}v_{\alpha},\\
&\square v^{\alpha}=-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma},\\
&v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w^{\alpha}=w^{\kappa}\partial^{\alpha}v_{\kappa}.\end{cases}

(2.7)

Our third result is as follows.

Theorem 2.3
.

Assume
0<\epsilon^{\prime}<\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{8}
. Let the state function
p(\varrho)=\varrho
and
\mathbf{w}
be defined in (
1.14
). Consider the Cauchy problem (
2.7
) with the initial data

(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})|_{t=0}=(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0}).

If
(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})\in H^{\frac{7}{4}+\epsilon}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{\frac{7}{4}+\epsilon}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{1+\epsilon^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, the Cauchy problem (
2.7
) is locally well-posed and
(dh,d\mathbf{v})\in L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}\cap L^{4}_{t}\dot{B}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{\infty,2}
.

Remark 2.3
.

(1)

In the case of a stiff fluid, the acoustic metric is the standard flat Minkowski metric. Therefore, the system (
2.7
) is a semilinear wave-transport system, which is different from the quasilinear wave-transport system (
1.19
). Therefore, we can directly obtain the Strichartz estimates for
h
and
\mathbf{v}_{+}
. Consequently, compared to theorems
2.1
and
2.2
, the required regularity of the vorticity can be reduced from
\frac{3}{2}+
to
1+
.

(2)

In Theorem
2.3
, the regularity of enthalpy and velocity corresponds to the sharp results of Smith and Tataru
[
61
]
, and the regularity of vorticity, c.f. Bourgain and Li
[
12
]
, is also sharp.

(3)

For the compressible Euler equations, there is no such good structure (
2.7
) in the particular state
p(\varrho)=\varrho
.

Furthermore, when the stiff fluid is irrotational, namely,
\mathbf{w}\equiv 0
, the system (
1.19
) becomes

\begin{cases}&\square h=-2\partial_{\beta}h\partial^{\beta}h-\mathrm{e}^{-2h}\partial_{\beta}v^{\alpha}\partial^{\beta}v_{\alpha},\\
&\square v^{\alpha}=0.\end{cases}

(2.8)

We find that there are “wave-map” null forms in the equation (
2.8
). Therefore, we can obtain the following result:

Theorem 2.4
.

Assume
0<\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{8}
. Let the state function
p(\varrho)=\varrho
and
\mathbf{w}\equiv 0
. Consider the Cauchy problem (
2.8
) with the initial data

(h,\mathbf{v})|_{t=0}=(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0}).

If
(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\in H^{1+\epsilon}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, the Cauchy problem (
2.8
) is locally well-posed. Moreover, if
(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\in\dot{B}_{2,1}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and is sufficiently small, the Cauchy problem (
2.8
) is globally well-posed.

Remark 2.4
.

For the system (
2.8
) satisfying “wave-map” null forms, therefore, we can derive Theorem
2.4
directly from the works of Klainerman-Machedon
[
38
]
, Klainerman-Selberg
[
39
]
, and Tataru
[
72
]
.

We express our gratitude to Lars Andersson and Changhua Wei for reminding us about the stiff case. The author is supported by the Natural Science Foundation of Hunan Province, China (Grant No. 2025JJ40003) and the Fundamental Research Funds for the Central Universities (Grant No. 531118010867).

2.3.
Organization of the rest paper

In Section
3
, we present preliminary work, including the derivation of new structures and energy estimates. In Section
4
, we provide a proof of Theorem
2.1
, assuming Proposition
4.2
. In Section
5
, we prove Proposition
4.2
under the assumptions of Propositions
5.2
and
5.3
. In Section
6
, we present a proof of Proposition
5.2
. In Section
7
, we prove Proposition
5.3
by assuming Proposition
7.1
. After that, in Section
8
, we prove Proposition
7.1
. In Section
9
, we provide a detailed proof of Theorem
2.2
. In Section
10
, we present proofs of Theorems
2.3
and
2.4
.

3.
New structures and energy estimates

In this section, we introduce several structures of the system (
1.11
) and prove some energy estimates.

3.1.
Hyperbolic reduction

Lemma 3.1

(
[
13
]
, Theorem 13.1, page 283)
.

Let
(\varrho,\mathbf{u})
be a solution of (
1.2
). Let
h
and
\mathbf{v}
be defined in (
1.8
) and (
1.9
). Denote
\mathbf{U}=(p(h),\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{1},\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{2})^{\mathrm{T}}
. Then System (
1.2
) can be reduced to the following symmetric hyperbolic equation:

A^{0}(\mathbf{U})\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}+A^{i}(\mathbf{U})\partial_{i}\mathbf{U}=0,

(3.1)

where

A^{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}(\varrho+p)^{-2}\varrho^{\prime}(p)\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{0}&-(\varrho+p)^{-1}\frac{v^{1}}{v_{0}}&-(\varrho+p)^{-1}\frac{v^{2}}{v_{0}}\\
-(\varrho+p)^{-1}\frac{v^{1}}{v_{0}}&\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{0}(1+\frac{v^{1}v_{1}}{v^{0}v_{0}})&\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{0}\frac{v^{1}v_{2}}{v^{0}v_{0}}\\
-(\varrho+p)^{-1}\frac{v^{2}}{v_{0}}&\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{0}\frac{v^{1}v_{2}}{v^{0}v_{0}}&\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{0}(1+\frac{v^{2}v_{2}}{v^{0}v_{0}})\\
\end{array}\right),

A^{1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}(\varrho+p)^{-2}\varrho^{\prime}(p)\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{1}&-(\varrho+p)^{-1}&0\\
-(\varrho+p)^{-1}&\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{1}(1+\frac{v^{1}v_{1}}{v^{0}v_{0}})&\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{1}\frac{v^{1}v_{2}}{v^{0}v_{0}}\\
0&\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{1}\frac{v^{1}v_{2}}{v^{0}v_{0}}&\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{1}(1+\frac{v^{2}v_{2}}{v^{0}v_{0}})\end{array}\right),

A^{2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}(\varrho+p)^{-2}\varrho^{\prime}(p)\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{2}&0&-(\varrho+p)^{-1}\\
0&\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{2}(1+\frac{v^{1}v_{1}}{v^{0}v_{0}})&\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{2}\frac{v^{1}v_{2}}{v^{0}v_{0}}\\
-(\varrho+p)^{-1}&\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{2}\frac{v^{1}v_{2}}{v^{0}v_{0}}&\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{2}(1+\frac{v^{2}v_{2}}{v^{0}v_{0}})\end{array}\right).

Remark 3.1
.

In the book
[
13
]
(Theorem 13.1, page 283), the hyperbolic system is formulated in terms of the variables
\varrho
and
\mathbf{u}
. In our paper, we transform these variables
(\varrho,\mathbf{u})
to
(h,\mathbf{v})
. Moreover, by applying equations (
1.3
), (
1.6
), (
1.7
), and (
1.8
), the functions
\varrho
,
p
and
\varrho^{\prime}(p)
are all depend on
h
. For other results on hyperbolic reduction, we also refer to
[
42
]
and
[
40
]
.

3.2.
Wave-transport structures

We begin by deriving (
1.11
).

Lemma 3.2
.

Under the settings (
1.5
)-(
1.8
), the system (
1.2
) can be written as (
1.11
).

Proof.

By using (
1.7
) and (
1.8
), we get

\begin{split}p+\varrho=q\mathrm{e}^{h}.\end{split}

(3.2)

Operating derivatives to
\varrho
for (
3.2
), we can see

\begin{split}p^{\prime}(\varrho)+1=\mathrm{e}^{h}\frac{dq}{d\varrho}+q\mathrm{e}^{h}\frac{dh}{d\varrho}.\end{split}

(3.3)

Inserting (
1.5
), (
1.6
) to (
3.3
), it yields

\begin{split}c^{2}_{s}+1=\mathrm{e}^{h}\frac{q}{p+\varrho}+q\mathrm{e}^{h}\frac{dh}{d\varrho}.\end{split}

(3.4)

Using (
3.2
), then (
3.4
) tells us that

\begin{split}c^{2}_{s}=q\mathrm{e}^{h}\frac{dh}{d\varrho}.\end{split}

(3.5)

From (
3.5
), we find

\begin{split}\partial_{\kappa}\varrho=c^{-2}_{s}q\mathrm{e}^{h}\partial_{\kappa}h.\end{split}

(3.6)

Using (
3.2
) and (
1.9
), we can derive that

\begin{split}(p+\varrho)\partial_{\kappa}u^{\kappa}=&q\mathrm{e}^{h}\partial_{\kappa}u^{\kappa}\\
=&q(\partial_{\kappa}v^{\kappa}-v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}h).\end{split}

(3.7)

From (
3.6
), we obtain

\begin{split}u^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\varrho=&c^{-2}_{s}q\mathrm{e}^{h}u^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}h\\
=&c^{-2}_{s}qv^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}h.\end{split}

(3.8)

Adding (
3.7
) and (
3.8
) yields

v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}h(c^{-2}_{s}-1)+\partial_{\kappa}v^{\kappa}=0.

(3.9)

Multiplying (
3.9
) with
c^{2}_{s}
, we therefore get

(1-c^{2}_{s})v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}h+c^{2}_{s}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\kappa}=0.

Similarly, we can calculate

\begin{split}(p+\varrho)\partial_{\kappa}u^{\kappa}=&q\mathrm{e}^{h}\partial_{\kappa}u^{\kappa},\\
=&q\mathrm{e}^{h}(v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\alpha}-v^{\alpha}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}h),\end{split}

(3.10)

and

\begin{split}(m^{\alpha\kappa}+u^{\alpha}u^{\kappa})\partial_{\kappa}p=&(m^{\alpha\kappa}+u^{\alpha}u^{\kappa})c^{2}_{s}\partial_{\kappa}\varrho\\
=&(m^{\alpha\kappa}+\mathrm{e}^{-2h}v^{\alpha}v^{\kappa})q\mathrm{e}^{h}\partial_{\kappa}h.\end{split}

(3.11)

Adding (
3.10
) and (
3.11
), we have shown

v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\alpha}+\mathrm{e}^{-2h}\partial^{\alpha}h=0.

(3.12)

Combining (
3.9
) and (
3.12
) yields the system (
1.11
).
∎

In the following lemma, we derive the new wave structure for the enthalpy and the rescaled velocity.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
(h,\mathbf{v})
be a solution of (
1.11
). Then we have

\begin{cases}&\square_{g}h\ =\mathcal{D},\\
&\square_{g}v^{\alpha}=-c^{2}_{s}\Theta\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}+Q^{\alpha},\end{cases}

where
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathbf{Q}=(Q^{0},Q^{1},Q^{2})
are quadratic terms defined as in (
1.20
) and (
1.21
).

Proof.

Operating the operator
v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}
on the first equation of (
1.11
), we have

v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}\left\{(1-c^{2}_{s})v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}h+c^{2}_{s}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\kappa}\right\}=0.

(3.13)

A direct calculation on (
3.13
) tells us

\begin{split}&(1-c^{2}_{s})v^{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial^{2}_{\beta\kappa}h+\underbrace{c^{2}_{s}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}(\partial_{\kappa}u^{\kappa})}_{\equiv R_{1}}+(1-c^{2}_{s})v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}h\\
&\quad-2c_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}v^{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\beta}h\partial_{\kappa}h+2c_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}h\partial_{\kappa}v^{\kappa}=0.\end{split}

(3.14)

For
R_{1}
, using (
1.11
), so we can compute it by

\begin{split}R_{1}=&c^{2}_{s}\partial_{\kappa}(v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa})-c^{2}_{s}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}\\
=&-c^{2}_{s}\partial_{\kappa}(\mathrm{e}^{2h}\partial^{\kappa}h)-c^{2}_{s}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}\\
=&-\mathrm{e}^{2h}c^{2}_{s}m^{\beta\kappa}\partial^{2}_{\beta\kappa}h-c^{2}_{s}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}-2\mathrm{e}^{2h}c^{2}_{s}\partial_{\kappa}h\partial^{\kappa}h.\end{split}

(3.15)

Substituting (
3.15
) to (
3.14
), and using (
1.11
) again, we obtain

\begin{split}&\left\{\mathrm{e}^{2h}c^{2}_{s}m^{\alpha\beta}+(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\alpha}v^{\beta}\right\}\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}h\\
=&(1-c^{2}_{s})v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}h-2c_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}v^{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\beta}h\partial_{\kappa}h+2c_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}h\partial_{\kappa}v^{\kappa}\\
&-c^{2}_{s}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}-2\mathrm{e}^{2h}c^{2}_{s}\partial_{\kappa}h\partial^{\kappa}h\\
=&-\mathrm{e}^{2h}(1+c^{2}_{s})\partial_{\kappa}h\partial^{\kappa}h-2c^{-1}_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}v^{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\beta}h\partial_{\kappa}h-c^{2}_{s}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}.\end{split}

(3.16)

To assure
g^{00}=-1
, multiplying (
3.16
) with
\mathrm{e}^{-2h}\Theta
, we can see

\begin{split}\square_{g}h=&-2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}\Theta c^{-1}_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}v^{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\beta}h\partial_{\kappa}h-\mathrm{e}^{-2h}\Theta c^{2}_{s}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}-\Theta(1+c^{2}_{s})\partial_{\kappa}h\partial^{\kappa}h.\end{split}

(3.17)

Operating
(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}
on the second equation in (
1.11
), we get

\begin{split}(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial^{2}_{\beta\kappa}v^{\alpha}+\underbrace{\mathrm{e}^{2h}(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}\partial^{\alpha}h}_{\equiv R_{2}}+(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\alpha}+2(c^{2}_{s}-1)\mathrm{e}^{2h}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}h\partial^{\alpha}h=0.\end{split}

(3.18)

Due to (
1.11
), we deduce that

\begin{split}R_{2}=&\mathrm{e}^{2h}\partial^{\alpha}\left\{(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}h\right\}-\mathrm{e}^{2h}(c^{2}_{s}-1)\partial^{\alpha}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}h-2c_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}\mathrm{e}^{2h}v^{\beta}\partial^{\alpha}h\partial_{\beta}h\\
=&\mathrm{e}^{2h}\partial^{\alpha}(c^{2}_{s}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\kappa})-\mathrm{e}^{2h}(c^{2}_{s}-1)\partial^{\alpha}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}h-2c_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}\mathrm{e}^{2h}v^{\beta}\partial^{\alpha}h\partial_{\beta}h\\
=&c^{2}_{s}\mathrm{e}^{2h}\partial_{\kappa}(\partial^{\alpha}v^{\kappa})+2c_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}\mathrm{e}^{2h}\partial^{\alpha}h\partial_{\kappa}v^{\kappa}-\mathrm{e}^{2h}(c^{2}_{s}-1)\partial^{\alpha}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}h-2c_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}\mathrm{e}^{2h}v^{\beta}\partial^{\alpha}h\partial_{\beta}h\end{split}

(3.19)

We also note

\begin{split}c^{2}_{s}\mathrm{e}^{2h}\partial_{\kappa}(\partial^{\alpha}v^{\kappa})=&c^{2}_{s}\mathrm{e}^{2h}\partial_{\kappa}(\partial^{\alpha}v^{\kappa}-\partial^{\kappa}v^{\alpha})+c^{2}_{s}\mathrm{e}^{2h}m^{\beta\kappa}\partial^{2}_{\beta\kappa}v^{\alpha}\\
=&c^{2}_{s}\mathrm{e}^{2h}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}+c^{2}_{s}\mathrm{e}^{2h}m^{\beta\kappa}\partial^{2}_{\beta\kappa}v^{\alpha}\end{split}

(3.20)

Summing up our outcome (
3.18
), (
3.19
), (
3.20
), we have

\begin{split}\left\{\mathrm{e}^{2h}c^{2}_{s}m^{\beta\kappa}+(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}v^{\kappa}\right\}\partial^{2}_{\beta\kappa}v^{\alpha}=&-c^{2}_{s}\mathrm{e}^{2h}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}-(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\alpha}\\
&-2(c^{2}_{s}-1)\mathrm{e}^{2h}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}h\partial^{\alpha}h-2c_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}\mathrm{e}^{2h}\partial^{\alpha}h\partial_{\kappa}v^{\kappa}\\
&+\mathrm{e}^{2h}(c^{2}_{s}-1)\partial^{\alpha}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}h+2c_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}\mathrm{e}^{2h}v^{\beta}\partial^{\alpha}h\partial_{\beta}h.\end{split}

(3.21)

Multiplying (
3.21
) with
\mathrm{e}^{-2h}\Theta
, then we have

\begin{split}\square_{{g}}v^{\alpha}=&-\Theta c^{2}_{s}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}-\mathrm{e}^{-2h}\Theta(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\alpha}\\
&-2\Theta(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}h\partial^{\alpha}h-2\Theta c_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}\partial^{\alpha}h\partial_{\kappa}v^{\kappa}\\
&+\Theta(c^{2}_{s}-1)\partial^{\alpha}v^{\beta}\partial_{\beta}h+2\Theta c_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}v^{\beta}\partial^{\alpha}h\partial_{\beta}h.\end{split}

(3.22)

Combining (
3.17
) and (
3.22
), this concludes the proof of this lemma.
∎

Next, we derive a good wave equation for
\mathbf{v}_{+}
.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
(h,\mathbf{v})
be a solution of (
1.11
). Let
\mathbf{v}_{+}
and
\mathbf{v}_{-}
be defined in (
1.18
). Then we have

\begin{split}\square_{{g}}\mathbf{v}_{+}=&\Theta\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(v^{0})^{2}(1-3c^{2}_{s})\mathbf{T}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}-c^{2}_{s}\Theta\mathbf{v}_{-}+\mathbf{Q},\end{split}

(3.23)

where
\Theta
,
\mathbf{Q}
, and
\mathbf{T}
are stated as in (
1.12
), (
1.21
), and (
1.22
).

Proof.

According to the expression of
\square_{{g}}
, we find that

\begin{split}\square_{g}v^{\alpha}=&g^{\beta\gamma}\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}v^{\alpha}=\Theta\left\{c^{2}_{s}m^{\beta\gamma}+\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}v^{\gamma}\right\}\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}v^{\alpha}.\end{split}

(3.24)

For
\mathbf{v}={\mathbf{v}}_{+}+\mathbf{v}_{-}
, the equation (
3.24
) becomes

\begin{split}\square_{g}v^{\alpha}=&\Theta\left\{c^{2}_{s}m^{\beta\gamma}+\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}v^{\gamma}\right\}\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}v_{+}^{\alpha}\\
&+\Theta\left\{c^{2}_{s}m^{\beta\gamma}+\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}v^{\gamma}\right\}\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}v_{-}^{\alpha}.\end{split}

(3.25)

Due to Lemma
1.1
, we also get

\square_{g}v^{\alpha}=-c^{2}_{s}\Theta\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}+Q^{\alpha}.

(3.26)

Combining (
3.25
) and (
3.26
) yield

\begin{split}\Theta\left\{c^{2}_{s}m^{\beta\gamma}+\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}v^{\gamma}\right\}\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}v_{+}^{\alpha}=&-\Theta\left\{c^{2}_{s}m^{\beta\gamma}+\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}v^{\gamma}\right\}\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}v_{-}^{\alpha}\\
&-c^{2}_{s}\Theta\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}+Q^{\alpha}.\end{split}

Using (
1.16
) and (
1.17
), we further get

\begin{split}&\Theta\left\{c^{2}_{s}m^{\beta\gamma}+\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}v^{\gamma}\right\}\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}v_{+}^{\alpha}\\
=&-\Theta\left\{c^{2}_{s}m^{\beta\gamma}+\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}v^{\gamma}\right\}\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}v_{-}^{\alpha}-c^{2}_{s}\Theta(\mathbf{{Id}}-\mathbf{{P}})v_{-}^{\alpha}+Q^{\alpha}\\
=&-\Theta\left\{c^{2}_{s}m^{\beta\gamma}+\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(c^{2}_{s}-1)v^{\beta}v^{\gamma}\right\}\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}v_{-}^{\alpha}-c^{2}_{s}\Theta\left\{v^{\alpha}_{-}-(m^{\beta\gamma}+2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}v^{\beta}v^{\gamma})\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}v^{\alpha}_{-}\right\}+Q^{\alpha}\\
=&\Theta(1-3c^{2}_{s})\mathrm{e}^{-2h}v^{\beta}v^{\gamma}\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}v^{\alpha}_{-}-c^{2}_{s}\Theta v^{\alpha}_{-}+Q^{\alpha}\\
=&\Theta\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(1-3c^{2}_{s})(v^{0})^{2}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{T}v^{\alpha}_{-}-c^{2}_{s}\Theta v^{\alpha}_{-}+Q^{\alpha}.\end{split}

Thus, we have proved (
3.23
). Therefore, we have finished the proof of this lemma.
∎

Remark 3.2
.

By comparing equations (
1.19
) and (
3.23
), we see that
\mathbf{v}_{+}
satisfies a better wave equation than
\mathbf{v}
. Moreover, the equation (
3.23
) plays a key role in establishing Strichartz estimates for
\mathbf{v}_{+}
.

Finally, we will derive two good transport formulations for vorticity.

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
(h,\mathbf{v})
be a solution of (
1.11
). Let
\mathbf{w}
and
\mathbf{W}
be defined in (
1.14
) and (
1.15
). Then we have the following two transport equations

v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w^{\alpha}=w^{\kappa}\partial^{\alpha}v_{\kappa}-w^{\alpha}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa},

(3.27)

and

\begin{split}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}W^{\alpha}=&-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w_{\gamma}-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}+\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\ \partial_{\beta}w^{\kappa}\partial_{\gamma}v_{\kappa}\\
&+\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\left\{(c^{-2}_{s}-1)v^{\kappa}\right\}\partial_{\kappa}h-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}(c^{-2}_{s}-1)v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}h\\
&+2\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}c^{-3}_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}v^{\kappa}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\kappa}h\partial_{\beta}h.\end{split}

(3.28)

Proof.

Operating
\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}
on the second equation of (
1.11
), we have

\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\left(v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}v_{\gamma}+\mathrm{e}^{2h}\partial_{\gamma}h\right)=0.

By chain’s rule, we further get

v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\left(\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}v_{\gamma}\right)+\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}v_{\gamma}+\underbrace{\mathrm{e}^{2h}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}h\partial_{\gamma}h}_{\equiv 0}+\underbrace{\mathrm{e}^{2h}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}h}_{\equiv 0}=0.

(3.29)

We also calculate

\begin{split}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}v_{\gamma}=&\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\partial_{\beta}v_{\kappa}-\partial_{\kappa}v_{\beta})\partial^{\kappa}v_{\gamma}+\underbrace{\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\beta}\partial_{\kappa}v_{\gamma}}_{\equiv 0}\\
=&\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\epsilon_{\mu\beta\kappa}w^{\mu}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\gamma}\\
=&-w^{\kappa}\partial^{\alpha}v_{\kappa}+w^{\alpha}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}.\end{split}

(3.30)

By using (
3.29
) and (
3.30
), we therefore get (
3.27
). Operating
\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}
on (
3.27
), we show that

\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}(v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w_{\gamma})=\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}(w^{\kappa}\partial_{\gamma}v_{\kappa})-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}(w_{\gamma}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}).

By chain’s rule, it yields

\begin{split}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}(\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma})=&-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w_{\gamma}-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}\\
&+\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\ \partial_{\beta}w^{\kappa}\partial_{\gamma}v_{\kappa}+\underbrace{\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w^{\kappa}\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}v_{\kappa}}_{\equiv 0}.\end{split}

Therefore, we obtain

\begin{split}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}(\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma})=&-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w_{\gamma}-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}+\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\ \partial_{\beta}w^{\kappa}\partial_{\gamma}v_{\kappa}.\end{split}

(3.31)

Taking advantage of (
1.11
), we can calculate

\begin{split}-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}=&\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\left\{(c^{-2}_{s}-1)v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}h\right\}\\
=&\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\left\{(c^{-2}_{s}-1)v^{\kappa}\right\}\partial_{\kappa}h+\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}(c^{-2}_{s}-1)w_{\gamma}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}(\partial_{\beta}h)\\
=&\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\left\{(c^{-2}_{s}-1)v^{\kappa}\right\}\partial_{\kappa}h+v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\left\{\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}(c^{-2}_{s}-1)w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}h\right\}\\
&-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}(c^{-2}_{s}-1)v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}h-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}(-2c^{-3}_{s}c^{\prime}_{s})v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}h\partial_{\beta}h.\end{split}

(3.32)

By (
3.31
) and (
3.32
), we therefore deduce

\begin{split}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}W^{\alpha}=&-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w_{\gamma}-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}+\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\ \partial_{\beta}w^{\kappa}\partial_{\gamma}v_{\kappa}+\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\left\{(c^{-2}_{s}-1)v^{\kappa}\right\}\partial_{\kappa}h\\
&-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}(c^{-2}_{s}-1)v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}h+2\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}c^{-3}_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}v^{\kappa}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\kappa}h\partial_{\beta}h.\end{split}

Therefore, we have finished the proof of (
3.28
). This concludes the proof of this lemma.
∎

3.3.
Commutator estimates and product estimates

Let us start by introducing a classical commutator estimate, which have been proved in the references
[
33
,
41
,
61
]
.

Lemma 3.6
.

[
33
]

Let
a\geq 0
. Then for any scalar function
f_{1},f_{2}
, we have

\|\Lambda_{x}^{a}(f_{1}f_{2})-(\Lambda_{x}^{a}f_{1})f_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|\Lambda_{x}^{a-1}f_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|\nabla f_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{p}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|\Lambda_{x}^{a}f_{2}\|_{L^{q}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

where
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{2}
.

Lemma 3.7

(
[
41
]
, Theorem 1.2)
.

Let
0<a<1
. Let
\mathbf{{P}}
be stated as in (
1.17
). Then we have

\|[\Lambda_{x}^{a},\mathbf{{P}}]f\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|\Lambda_{x}^{a}\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|d^{2}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

Next, let us introduce some product estimates.

Lemma 3.8
.

[
33
]

Let
F(f)
be a smooth function of
u
,
F(0)=0
and
f\in L^{\infty}_{x}
. For any
a\geq 0
, we have

\|F(f)\|_{H^{a}}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{a}}(1+\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}).

Lemma 3.9
.

[
61
]

Suppose that
0\leq r,r^{\prime}<\frac{n}{2}
and
r+r^{\prime}>\frac{n}{2}
. Then

\|hf\|_{H^{r+r^{\prime}-\frac{n}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C_{r,r^{\prime}}\|h\|_{H^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|f\|_{H^{r^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

Moreover, if
-r\leq r^{\prime}\leq r
and
r>\frac{n}{2}
, then the following estimate holds:

\|hf\|_{H^{r^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C_{r,r^{\prime}}\|h\|_{H^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\|f\|_{H^{r^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

We next prove a product estimate.

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
0<a<1
. Then we have

\|\Lambda_{x}^{a}(f_{1}f_{2})\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\|f_{1}\|_{\dot{B}^{a}_{\infty,2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|f_{2}\|_{\dot{H}^{a}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Proof.

Due to Littlewood-Paley decomposition, we have

\begin{split}P_{j}(f_{1}f_{2})=&P_{j}\big(\sum_{|k-j|\leq 2}S_{k-1}f_{1}P_{k}f_{2}\big)+P_{j}\big(\sum_{|k-j|\leq 2}S_{k-1}f_{2}P_{k}f_{1}\big)+P_{j}\big(\sum_{k\geq j-1}P_{k}f_{1}P_{k}f_{2}\big),\end{split}

where
S_{k-1}=\sum_{j^{\prime}\leq k-1}P_{j^{\prime}}
. Due to Hölder’s inequality, it follows

\begin{split}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{2ja}\|P_{j}\big(\sum_{|k-j|\leq 2}S_{k-1}f_{1}P_{k}f_{2}\big)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim&\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{2ja}\|P_{j}f_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|f_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|f_{2}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{a}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|f_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.\end{split}

(3.33)

Similarly, we can obtain

\begin{split}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{2ja}\|P_{j}\big(\sum_{|k-j|\leq 2}S_{k-1}f_{2}P_{k}f_{1}\big)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim&\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{2ja}\|P_{j}f_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|f_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\\
\lesssim&\|f_{1}\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{a}_{\infty,2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|f_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.\end{split}

(3.34)

For the rest term, owing to Hölder’s inequality, it yields

\begin{split}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{2ja}\|P_{j}\big(\sum_{k\geq j-1}P_{k}f_{1}P_{k}f_{2}\big)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim&\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{2ka}\|P_{k}f_{1}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|P_{k}f_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\sum_{j\leq k}2^{2(j-k)a}\\
\lesssim&\|f_{1}\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{a}_{\infty,2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|f_{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.\end{split}

(3.35)

Combing (
3.33
), (
3.34
), and (
3.35
), we have proved this lemma.
∎

3.4.
Elliptic operator and estimates

In (
1.17
), we define a new second-order operator
\mathbf{P}=(m^{\beta\gamma}+2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}v^{\beta}v^{\gamma})\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}
. In the following, we will verify that
\mathbf{P}
is a uniformly elliptic operator.

Lemma 3.11
.

Let
(h,\mathbf{v})
be a solution of (
1.11
) and
(h,\mathbf{v})\in C([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
, and assume that (
1.23
) holds.
Let
\mathbf{P}
be stated as in (
1.17
). Then
\mathbf{P}
is a uniformly elliptic operator on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
Furthermore, for
F\in L^{2}([0,T];L_{x}^{2})
, consider the elliptic equation:

(\mathbf{Id}-\mathbf{P})f=F.

Then there is a unique solution
f\in H^{2}_{t,x}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
and

\|f\|_{H^{2}_{t,x}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})}\lesssim\|F\|_{L_{[0,T]}^{2}L_{x}^{2}},

(3.36)

where

H^{2}_{t,x}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})=\left\{f(t,x):\partial^{a}_{t}\partial_{x}^{b}f\in L^{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}),a\geq 0,b\geq 0,a+b\leq 2\right\},

and

\|f\|_{H^{2}_{t,x}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})}=\sum_{a\geq 0,b\geq 0,a+b\leq 2}\|\partial^{a}_{t}\partial_{x}^{b}f\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}L^{2}_{x}}.

Proof.

We first calculate the matrix
\mathrm{P}=(\mathrm{P}^{\beta\gamma})_{3\times 3}
, where
\mathrm{P}^{\beta\gamma}=m^{\beta\gamma}+2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}v^{\beta}v^{\gamma}
. Let us pick three leading principal minors

\begin{split}p_{1}=\begin{matrix}-1+2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(v^{0})^{2}\end{matrix},\qquad p_{2}=\left|\begin{matrix}-1+2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(v^{0})^{2}&2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}v^{0}v^{1}\\
2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}v^{0}v^{1}&1+2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(v^{1})^{2}\\
\end{matrix}\right|,\end{split}

and

\begin{split}p_{3}=\left|\begin{matrix}-1+2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(v^{0})^{2}&2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}v^{0}v^{1}&2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}v^{0}v^{2}\\
2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}v^{0}v^{1}&1+2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(v^{1})^{2}&2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}v^{1}v^{2}\\
2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}v^{0}v^{2}&2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}v^{1}v^{2}&1+2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(v^{2})^{2}\\
\end{matrix}\right|.\end{split}

Using
\mathrm{e}^{-2h}m_{\alpha\beta}v^{\alpha}v^{\beta}=-1
, it follows

(v^{0})^{2}=\mathrm{e}^{2h}+(v^{1})^{2}+(v^{2})^{2}.

(3.37)

For
p_{1}
, using (
3.37
), we get

\begin{split}p_{1}=-1+2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(v^{0})^{2}\geq 1.\end{split}

(3.38)

Due to (
3.37
), a direct calculation yields

\begin{split}p_{2}=&-1+2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}\{(v^{0})^{2}-(v^{1})^{2}\}\geq 1,\\
p_{3}=&-1+2\mathrm{e}^{-2h}\{(v^{0})^{2}-(v^{1})^{2}-(v^{2})^{2}\}\geq 1.\end{split}

(3.39)

By applying (
3.38
) and (
3.39
),
\mathbf{P}
is a positive definite matrix. Therefore,
\mathbf{P}
is a uniformly elliptic operator.

Since we are only considering the Cauchy problem, no boundary conditions are added to solve equation (
3.11
). Therefore, we extend
h
,
\mathbf{v}
,
F
, and
\mathbf{{P}}
from
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
to
\mathbb{R}^{1+2}
. Let

\bar{h}(t,x)=\begin{cases}&0,\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad t\in(-\infty,-T),\\
&(1+\frac{t}{T})h(-t,x),\ \quad t\in[-T,0),\\
&h(t,x),\qquad\qquad\ \ \ t\in[0,T],\\
&(2-\frac{t}{T})h(2T-t,x),\quad t\in(T,2T),\\
&0,\quad\qquad\qquad\ \ \ \ \ \ \ t\in(2T,+\infty).\end{cases}

and

\bar{\mathbf{v}}(t,x)=\begin{cases}&0,\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad t\in(-\infty,-T),\\
&(1+\frac{t}{T})\mathbf{v}(-t,x),\ \quad t\in[-T,0),\\
&\mathbf{v}(t,x),\qquad\qquad\ \ \ t\in[0,T],\\
&(2-\frac{t}{T})\mathbf{v}(2T-t,x),\quad t\in(T,2T),\\
&0,\quad\qquad\qquad\ \ \ \ \ \ \ t\in(2T,+\infty).\end{cases}

and

\bar{F}(t,x)=\begin{cases}&0,\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad t\in(-\infty,-T),\\
&(1+\frac{t}{T})F(-t,x),\ \quad t\in[-T,0),\\
&F(t,x),\qquad\qquad\ \ \ t\in[0,T],\\
&(2-\frac{t}{T})F(2T-t,x),\quad t\in(T,2T),\\
&0,\quad\qquad\qquad\ \ \ \ \ \ \ t\in(2T,+\infty).\end{cases}

(3.40)

We also set

\mathbf{\bar{P}}=(m^{\alpha\beta}+2\mathrm{e}^{-2\bar{h}}\bar{v}^{\alpha}\bar{v}^{\beta})\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}.

Consider the problem

(\mathbf{Id}-\mathbf{\bar{P}})\bar{f}=\bar{F}.

(3.41)

Note
\mathbf{\bar{P}}|_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}=\mathbf{{P}}
and

(\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{v}},\bar{F})|_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}=({h},{\mathbf{v}},{F}).

According to the uniqueness of solutions, we therefore obtain

\bar{f}|_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}=f.

(3.42)

For (
3.41
), using
[
23
]
(Lemma 9.17, page 255), we derive

\begin{split}\|\bar{f}\|_{H^{2}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}^{1+2})}\leq&\|\bar{F}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}^{1+2})}.\end{split}

(3.43)

Due to (
3.40
), we can calculate

\begin{split}\|\bar{F}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}^{1+2})}=&\int^{0}_{-T}\|(1+\frac{t}{T})F(-t,x)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}dt+\int_{0}^{T}\|F(t,x)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}dt\\
&+\int^{2T}_{T}\|(2-\frac{t}{T})F(2T-t,x)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}dt\\
\lesssim&\int^{0}_{-T}\|F(-t,x)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}dt+\int_{0}^{T}\|F(t,x)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}dt+\int^{2T}_{T}\|F(2T-t,x)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}dt\\
\lesssim&\|{F}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.44)

By using (
3.42
), we have

\|{f}\|_{H^{2}_{t,x}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\|\bar{f}\|_{H^{2}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}^{1+2})}.

(3.45)

Combining (
3.43
), (
3.44
), and (
3.45
), we obtain the estimate (
3.36
). Therefore, we complete the proof of this lemma.
∎

Based on the above lemma
3.11
, we can obtain the following result:

Lemma 3.12
.

Assume
s\in(\frac{7}{4},\frac{15}{8}]
. Let
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
be a solution of (
1.19
) on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Let
\mathbf{v}_{-}
and
\mathbf{T}
be defined as in (
1.16
) and (
1.22
). Then the function
\mathbf{v}_{-}
satisfies the following estimates

\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{1+a}}+\|\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{2+a}}\lesssim(1+\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}})\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{1+a}},\quad 0\leq a<s-1,

(3.46)

and

\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s-1}}+\|\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{1+a}}(1+\|h,\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}),\quad a>0.

(3.47)

Proof.

Noting that (
1.16
), and using Lemma
3.11
, it yields

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{2}}\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{1}}.\end{split}

(3.48)

Operating
\Lambda_{x}^{a}
on (
1.16
), we deduce

\begin{split}(\mathbf{{Id}}-\mathbf{{P}})\Lambda_{x}^{a}v^{\alpha}_{-}=\Lambda_{x}^{a}\left(\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}\right)+[\Lambda_{x}^{a},\mathbf{{P}}]v^{\alpha}_{-}.\end{split}

Using
3.11
, for
0\leq a<s-1
and
s>\frac{7}{4}
, we can derive that

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}\Lambda_{x}^{a}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{1}}+\|\Lambda_{x}^{a}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{2}}\lesssim&\|\Lambda_{x}^{a}(d\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}+\|[\Lambda_{x}^{a},\mathbf{{P}}]\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{1+a}}++\|[\Lambda_{x}^{a},\mathbf{{P}}]\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

(3.49)

Using Lemma
3.7
and (
3.48
), it follows

\begin{split}\|[\Lambda_{x}^{a},\mathbf{{P}}]\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim&\|\Lambda_{x}^{a}h,\Lambda_{x}^{a}\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d^{2}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\\
\lesssim&\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{1}}.\end{split}

(3.50)

Substituting (
3.50
) to (
3.49
), we therefore prove (
3.46
). Due to (
1.16
), we can derive

\begin{split}\mathbf{{P}}(\mathbf{T}v^{\alpha}_{-})=&-\mathbf{T}\big(c^{2}_{s}\Theta\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}\big)+[\mathbf{T},\mathbf{{P}}]v^{\alpha}_{-}\\
=&-\mathbf{T}\big(c^{2}_{s}\Theta\big)\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}-c^{2}_{s}\Theta\mathbf{T}\left\{W^{\alpha}-(1-c^{-2}_{s})\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}h\right\}+[\mathbf{T},\mathbf{{P}}]v^{\alpha}_{-}\\
=&-\mathbf{T}\big(c^{2}_{s}\Theta\big)\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}-c^{2}_{s}\Theta\mathbf{T}W^{\alpha}+c^{2}_{s}\Theta\mathbf{T}\left\{(1-c^{-2}_{s})\right\}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}h\\
&-[\mathbf{T},\mathbf{{P}}]v^{\alpha}_{-}+c^{2}_{s}(1-c^{-2}_{s})\Theta\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}h\mathbf{T}w_{\gamma}+c^{2}_{s}(1-c^{-2}_{s})\Theta\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}\mathbf{T}(\partial_{\beta}h).\end{split}

(3.51)

We set
\mathbf{{\Gamma}}=({\Gamma}^{0},{\Gamma}^{1},{\Gamma}^{2})
and

\begin{split}{\Gamma}^{\alpha}=&-\mathbf{T}\big(c^{2}_{s}\Theta\big)\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}-c^{2}_{s}\Theta\mathbf{T}W^{\alpha}+c^{2}_{s}\Theta\mathbf{T}\left\{(1-c^{-2}_{s})\right\}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}h\\
&-[\mathbf{T},\mathbf{{P}}]v^{\alpha}_{-}+c^{2}_{s}(1-c^{-2}_{s})\Theta\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}h\mathbf{T}w_{\gamma}+c^{2}_{s}(1-c^{-2}_{s})\Theta\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}\mathbf{T}(\partial_{\beta}h).\end{split}

Seeing (
3.28
), then
\mathbf{T}\mathbf{W}
formulates by
(d\mathbf{v},dh)\cdot d\mathbf{w}
and
\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)
. On the other hand,
[\mathbf{T},\mathbf{{P}}]\mathbf{v}_{-}
expresses by
(dh,d\mathbf{v})\cdot d^{2}\mathbf{v}_{-}
and
(d^{2}h,d^{2}\mathbf{v})\cdot d\mathbf{v}_{-}
. These imply that
\mathbf{\Gamma}
includes the following terms:

(d\mathbf{v},dh)\cdot d\mathbf{w},\quad\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh),\quad(dh,d\mathbf{v})\cdot d^{2}\mathbf{v}_{-},\quad(d^{2}h,d^{2}\mathbf{v})\cdot d\mathbf{v}_{-},\quad\mathbf{w}\cdot d^{2}h.

Due to (
3.51
), we have

\begin{split}\mathbf{{P}}\left(\Lambda^{s-2}_{x}(\mathbf{T}v^{\alpha}_{-})\right)=&\Lambda^{s-2}_{x}\Gamma^{\alpha}+[\mathbf{{P}},\Lambda^{s-2}_{x}]\mathbf{T}v^{\alpha}_{-}.\end{split}

(3.52)

Applying Lemma
3.11
on (
3.52
), we obtain

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}\Lambda^{s-2}_{x}(\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1}_{x}}+\|\Lambda^{s-2}_{x}(\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\Lambda^{s-2}_{x}\mathbf{{\Gamma}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|[\mathbf{{P}},\Lambda^{s-2}_{x}]\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.53)

Note that
s\in(\frac{7}{4},\frac{15}{8}]
and
0<a<s-1
. Using Hölder’s inequality along with (
3.46
), we can deduce

\begin{split}&\|\Lambda^{s-2}_{x}\mathbf{{\Gamma}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|(d\mathbf{v},dh)\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}}\\
&+\|(d\mathbf{v},dh)\cdot d^{2}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}}+\|(d^{2}h,d^{2}\mathbf{v})\cdot d\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot d^{2}h\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{1+a}}(1+\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}).\end{split}

(3.54)

We next claim that

\begin{split}\|[\Lambda^{s-2}_{x},\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla^{2}]\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{v}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\Lambda^{s-1-a}_{x}\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{H^{1+a}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.55)

To verify (
3.55
), due to Little-wood Paley decomposition, we obtain

\begin{split}P_{j}[\Lambda_{x}^{s-2},\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla^{2}]\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}=&I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3},\end{split}

(3.56)

where
S_{k-1}=\sum_{j^{\prime}\leq k-1}P_{j^{\prime}}
and

\begin{split}I_{1}=&\sum_{|k-j|\leq 2}P_{j}\left\{\Lambda_{x}^{s-2}\left(P_{k}\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla^{2}S_{k-1}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\right)-P_{k}\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla^{2}\Lambda_{x}^{s-2}S_{k-1}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\right\},\\
I_{2}=&\sum_{|k-j|\leq 2}P_{j}\left\{\Lambda_{x}^{s-2}\left(S_{k-1}\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla^{2}P_{k}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\right)-S_{k-1}\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla^{2}\Lambda_{x}^{s-2}P_{k}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\right\},\\
I_{3}=&\sum_{k\geq j-1}P_{j}\left\{\Lambda_{x}^{s-2}\left(P_{k}\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla^{2}P_{k}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\right)-P_{k}\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla^{2}\Lambda_{x}^{s-2}P_{k}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\right\}.\end{split}

By applying Bernstein estimate and Hölder’s inequality, we derive

\|I_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|I_{3}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim 2^{js}\|P_{j}\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}.

(3.57)

Consider

\begin{split}&\Lambda_{x}^{s-2}\left(S_{k-1}\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla^{2}P_{k}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\right)-S_{k-1}\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla^{2}\Lambda_{x}^{s-2}P_{k}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\\
=&\Lambda_{x}^{s-2}\nabla\left(S_{k-1}\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla P_{k}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\right)-\Lambda_{x}^{s-2}(\nabla S_{k-1}\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla P_{k}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-})-S_{k-1}\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla^{2}\Lambda_{x}^{s-2}P_{k}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\\
=&[\Lambda_{x}^{s-2}\nabla,S_{k-1}\mathbf{v}]\nabla P_{k}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}-\Lambda_{x}^{s-2}(\nabla S_{k-1}\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla P_{k}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}).\end{split}

We therefore can write

\begin{split}I_{2}=&\sum_{|k-j|\leq 2}P_{j}\left([\Lambda_{x}^{s-2}\nabla,S_{k-1}\mathbf{v}]\nabla P_{k}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\right)-\sum_{|k-j|\leq 2}P_{j}\left\{\Lambda_{x}^{s-2}(\nabla S_{k-1}\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla P_{k}\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-})\right\}.\end{split}

Applying classical commutator estimate, Bernstein estimate and Hölder’s inequality, we can obtain

\begin{split}\|I_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim&\sum_{|k-j|\leq 2}\|\Lambda_{x}^{s-1}S_{k-1}\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\cdot\|\Delta_{k}\nabla\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.\\
\lesssim&\|\Lambda_{x}^{s-1-a}\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\cdot 2^{ja}\|\Delta_{j}\nabla\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.58)

By using (
3.56
), (
3.57
), and (
3.58
), and taking
l^{2}
norm for
j
, we finally obtain (
3.55
). Similarly, we can also conclude
1
1

1

If it concerns
\partial^{2}_{tt}\mathbf{v}_{-}
, we can use (
1.16
) to transfer it to
\nabla\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}
and
\nabla^{2}\mathbf{v}_{-}
.

\begin{split}\|[\Lambda^{s-2}_{x},\mathbf{v}\partial^{2}_{tt}]\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{v}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\Lambda^{s-1-a}_{x}\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{H^{1+a}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.59)

and

\begin{split}\|[\Lambda^{s-2}_{x},\mathbf{v}\cdot\partial_{t}\nabla]\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{v}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\Lambda^{s-1-a}_{x}\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{H^{1+a}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.60)

Since (
3.55
), (
3.59
), and (
3.60
), we can obtain

\begin{split}\|[\Lambda^{s-2}_{x},\mathbf{{P}}]\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{v}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}(\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})\\
&+\|\Lambda^{s-1-a}_{x}\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1+a}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1+a}_{x}}).\end{split}

(3.61)

By applying Soblev’s imbeddings and (
3.46
), we have

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{1+a}}(1+\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}).\end{split}

(3.62)

Due to Soblev’s imbeddings again, we derive

\begin{split}\|\Lambda^{s-1-a}_{x}\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}.\end{split}

(3.63)

Using (
3.46
) again, we obtain

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1+a}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1+a}_{x}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{1+a}}(1+\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}).\end{split}

(3.64)

Gathering the estimates (
3.61
), (
3.62
), (
3.63
), and (
3.64
), it yields

\begin{split}\|[\mathbf{{P}},\Lambda^{s-2}_{x}]\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{1+a}}(1+\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}).\end{split}

(3.65)

Combining inequalities (
3.53
), (
3.54
), and (
3.65
), we obtain (
3.47
). This concludes the proof of this lemma.
∎

3.5.
Energy estimates

We will first introduce a classical energy estimate for quasi-linear hyperbolic systems, as presented in Li and Qin’s book
[
42
]
.

Lemma 3.13
.

[
42
]

Let
a\geq 0
and
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
be a solution of (
1.19
) on
[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Assume

\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,t]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq 2+C_{0}.

(3.66)

Then we have the integral inequality

\|(h,\mathbf{v})\|^{2}_{H^{a}_{x}}\lesssim\|(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\|^{2}_{H^{a}_{x}}+\int^{t}_{0}\|(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|(h,\mathbf{v})\|^{2}_{H^{a}_{x}}d\tau,

(3.67)

and the energy inequality

\|(h,\mathbf{v})\|_{H^{a}_{x}}\lesssim\|(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\|_{H^{a}_{x}}\exp\left(\int^{t}_{0}\|(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\right).

(3.68)

Next, we will derive the total energy estimates concerning enthalpy, velocity, and vorticity as follows.

Theorem 3.14
.

Let
\frac{7}{4}\leq s^{\prime}\leq s\leq 2
and
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
be a solution of (
1.19
) on
[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Assume (
3.66
) hold. Denote

\begin{split}E(t)=\|h\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}.\end{split}

When
0<t\leq 1
, We have

\begin{split}E(t)\lesssim&(1+E^{5}(0))\exp\left(\big[1+E^{3}(0)\int^{t}_{0}\|(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\big]\cdot\big[\int^{t}_{0}\|(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau+1\big]\right).\end{split}

Proof.

By using (
3.67
), we only need to bound
\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}
and
\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}
. To be simple, we set

\mathring{\mathbf{w}}=(w^{1},w^{2}).

Step 1:
\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}
. For
\mathbf{w}=(w^{0},\mathring{\mathbf{w}})
, we get

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}=&\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}.\end{split}

By elliptic estimate, one can see

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}=&\|w^{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\dot{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}\\
=&\|w^{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}+\|\textrm{curl}\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\dot{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}+\|\textrm{div}\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\dot{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}.\end{split}

Due to (
1.14
), we have

\partial_{\alpha}w^{\alpha}=0.

(3.69)

Therefore, (
3.69
) implies that

\textrm{div}\mathring{\mathbf{w}}=\partial_{\alpha}w^{\alpha}-\partial_{t}w^{0}=-\partial_{t}w^{0}.

Due to (
3.27
), we have

\partial_{t}w^{\alpha}+(v^{0})^{-1}v^{i}\partial_{i}w^{\alpha}=(v^{0})^{-1}w^{\kappa}\partial^{\alpha}v_{\kappa}-(v^{0})^{-1}w^{\alpha}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}.

(3.70)

Then we can deduce that

\textrm{div}\mathring{\mathbf{w}}=(v^{0})^{-1}v^{i}\partial_{i}w^{0}+(v^{0})^{-1}w^{\kappa}\partial_{t}v_{\kappa}+(v^{0})^{-1}w^{0}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}.

(3.71)

On the other hand, a direct calculation shows

\begin{split}\epsilon_{\alpha i0}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}=&(\delta^{\beta}_{i}\delta^{\gamma}_{0}-\delta^{\beta}_{0}\delta^{\gamma}_{i})\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}\\
=&\partial_{i}w_{0}-\partial_{t}w_{i}.\end{split}

(3.72)

By use of (
3.70
) and (
3.72
), we have

\begin{split}\partial_{i}w_{0}=&\epsilon_{\alpha i0}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}-(v^{0})^{-1}v^{j}\partial_{j}w_{i}+(v^{0})^{-1}w^{\kappa}\partial_{i}v_{\kappa}-(v^{0})^{-1}w_{i}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}\\
=&\epsilon_{\alpha i0}W^{\alpha}-(1-c^{-2}_{s})(w_{i}\partial_{t}h-w_{0}\partial_{i}h)-(v^{0})^{-1}v^{j}\partial_{j}w_{i}\\
&+(v^{0})^{-1}w^{\kappa}\partial_{i}v_{\kappa}-(v^{0})^{-1}w_{i}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}.\end{split}

(3.73)

Combing (
3.71
) and (
3.73
), we further get

\begin{split}\textrm{div}\mathring{\mathbf{w}}=&(v^{0})^{-1}v^{i}\epsilon_{\alpha i0}W^{\alpha}-(v^{0})^{-2}v^{i}v^{j}\partial_{j}w_{i}+(v^{0})^{-2}v^{i}w^{\kappa}\partial_{i}v_{\kappa}\\
&-(v^{0})^{-2}v^{i}w_{i}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}+(v^{0})^{-1}w^{\kappa}\partial_{t}v_{\kappa}+(v^{0})^{-1}w^{0}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}\\
&-(1-c^{-2}_{s})(w_{i}\partial_{t}h-w_{0}\partial_{i}h).\end{split}

(3.74)

From (
3.74
), we know

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{div}\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}\leq&C\|\mathbf{W}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}+(\frac{|\mathring{\mathbf{v}}|}{v^{0}})^{2}\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}+C\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.75)

By the definition (
1.15
) for
\mathbf{W}
, we can see

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{curl}\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}\leq&\|\mathbf{W}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.76)

Taking advantage of (
3.75
) and (
3.76
), we get

\left\{1-(\frac{|\mathring{\mathbf{v}}|}{v^{0}})^{2}\right\}\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\dot{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{W}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}.

(3.77)

By use of (
1.10
) and (
3.66
), then (
3.77
) becomes

\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\dot{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{W}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}.

(3.78)

From (
3.73
) and (
3.78
), we obtain

\begin{split}\|w^{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{W}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{W}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.79)

Combing (
3.78
) and (
3.79
), we therefore get

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{W}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}.\end{split}

Multiplying
w^{\alpha}
on (
3.70
), integrating it on
[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and summing
\alpha
over
0
to
2
, it yields

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\int^{t}_{0}\left(\|(v^{0})^{-1}d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|(v^{0})^{-2}\mathbf{v}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\right)\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau.\end{split}

(3.80)

By (
3.28
), we get

\begin{split}(\partial_{t}+(v^{0})^{-1}v^{i}\partial_{i})W^{\alpha}=&-(v^{0})^{-1}\left\{\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}(c^{-2}_{s}-1)v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}h+\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\left[(c^{-2}_{s}-1)v^{\kappa}\right]\partial_{\kappa}h\right\}\\
