&\ -\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}(v^{0})^{-1}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}+2\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}(v^{0})^{-1}c^{-3}_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}v^{\kappa}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\kappa}h\partial_{\beta}h\\
&\ \ -\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}(v^{0})^{-1}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w_{\gamma}+\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}(v^{0})^{-1}\partial_{\beta}w^{\kappa}\partial_{\gamma}v_{\kappa}.\end{split}

(3.81)

Seeing the right hand side of (
3.81
), it includes two types terms:

(dh,d\mathbf{v})\cdot d\mathbf{w},\quad\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v}).

Operating
\Lambda_{x}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}
on (
3.81
), multiplying
\Lambda_{x}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}W^{\alpha}
, integrating it om
[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and summing
\alpha
over
0
to
2
, we can derive that

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{W}\|^{2}_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}\lesssim&\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|^{2}_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}d\tau+\int^{t}_{0}\|(dh,d\mathbf{v})\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}}+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}d\tau.\end{split}

(3.82)

On the right hand side of (
3.82
), we have

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\|_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\cdot\nabla h_{0}\|_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}+\|(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\|^{2}_{{H}^{s}}.\end{split}

(3.83)

and

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot dh\|_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}+\|(h,\mathbf{v})\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{s}}.\end{split}

(3.84)

Utilizing (
3.82
), (
3.83
), (
3.84
), and Hölder’s inequality, we obtain

\begin{split}\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}+\|(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\|^{4}_{{H}^{s}}+\int^{t}_{0}\|(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|(h,\mathbf{v})\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}d\tau+\int^{t}_{0}\|(h,\mathbf{v})\|^{3}_{H^{s}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{{H}^{s^{\prime}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}d\tau.\end{split}

(3.85)

Step 2:
\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}
. By Hodge’s decomposition, we have

\begin{split}\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}=\|\nabla w^{0}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|\nabla\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.86)

By using (
3.73
), we can obtain

\begin{split}\|\nabla w^{0}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|\nabla\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{L^{8}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.87)

Due to (
3.74
), it yields

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{div}\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}\leq&C\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}+(\frac{|\mathring{\mathbf{v}}|}{v^{0}})^{2}\|\nabla\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}+C\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{L^{8}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.88)

By using (
1.15
), it follows

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{curl}\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}\leq&\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{L^{8}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.89)

Taking advantage of (
3.88
) and (
3.89
), we get

\begin{split}\left\{1-(\frac{|\mathring{\mathbf{v}}|}{v^{0}})^{2}\right\}\|\nabla\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}\leq&C\|\mathbf{W}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}+C\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{L^{8}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.90)

By using (
1.10
) and (
3.66
), so (
3.90
) can be calculated by

\|\nabla\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{L^{8}_{x}}.

(3.91)

Summing up the outcome (
3.86
), (
3.87
), (
3.91
), we therefore derive

\|\nabla{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{L^{8}_{x}}.

Multiplying
W^{\alpha}|\mathbf{W}|^{6}
on (
3.81
) and integrating it on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and summing
\alpha
over
0
to
2
, we can derive that

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L_{x}^{8}}^{8}\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|^{8}_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{v}\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|^{7}_{L^{8}_{x}}\\
&+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{8}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|^{7}_{L^{8}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.92)

Due to (
3.92
), it yields

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L_{x}^{8}}^{2}\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|^{2}_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{v}\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}\\
&+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{8}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.93)

Integrating (
3.93
) from
0
to
t
, we can deduce that

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L_{x}^{8}}^{2}-\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{8}}^{2}\lesssim&\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|^{2}_{L^{8}_{x}}d\tau+\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{v}\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{8}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}d\tau.\end{split}

(3.94)

On the left side of (
3.94
), we have

\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{8}}^{2}\leq\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{8}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\cdot\nabla h_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{8}}^{2}\leq\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{8}}^{2}+\|(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\|^{4}_{H^{s}}.

(3.95)

Combining (
3.94
), (
3.95
), and using (
1.15
), it turns out

\begin{split}&\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{8}_{x}}-\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{8}_{x}}-(\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{8}}^{2}+\|(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\|^{4}_{H^{s}})\\
\lesssim&+\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{8}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{v}\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}(\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{8}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{8}_{x}}(\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{8}_{x}})d\tau.\end{split}

(3.96)

For
\frac{7}{4}\leq s^{\prime}\leq s\leq 2
, using Hölder’s inequality, we can derive

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{8}_{x}}\leq&\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{H^{\frac{3}{4}}_{x}}\\
\leq&\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{4}}}\|(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{H^{\frac{3}{4}}_{x}}.\end{split}

Using interpolation formula and Young’s inequality, we can estimate
\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{8}_{x}}
by

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{8}_{x}}\leq&\|\mathbf{w}\|^{\frac{2}{3}}_{H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{\frac{1}{3}}_{H_{x}^{s_{0}-1/4}}\|(\mathbf{v},h)\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\\
\leq&\frac{1}{4}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}-1/4}}+C\|\mathbf{w}\|^{\frac{2}{3}}_{H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}\|(\mathbf{v},h)\|^{\frac{3}{2}}_{H^{s}_{x}}\\
\leq&\frac{1}{4}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}-1/4}}+C\|(\mathbf{v},h)\|^{\frac{5}{2}}_{H^{s}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.97)

Due to (
3.96
) and (
3.97
), we can get

\begin{split}&\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{8}_{x}}-\frac{1}{4}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}\\
\lesssim&\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{8}}^{2}+\|(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\|^{4}_{H^{s}}+\|(\mathbf{v},h)\|^{5}_{H^{s}_{x}}\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{8}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{v}\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}(\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{8}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{8}_{x}}(\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{8}_{x}})d\tau.\end{split}

(3.98)

By (
3.98
), using Hölder’s inequality, we finally obtain

\begin{split}&\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{8}_{x}}-\frac{1}{4}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}\\
\lesssim&\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{8}}^{2}+\|(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0})\|^{4}_{H^{s}}+\|(\mathbf{v},h)\|^{5}_{H^{s}_{x}}\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{{H}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}\|(\mathbf{v},h)\|^{2}_{{H}^{s}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}(\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}\|(\mathbf{v},h)\|_{{H}^{s}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}\|(\mathbf{v},h)\|^{2}_{{H}^{s}_{x}}(\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{8}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}\|(\mathbf{v},h)\|_{{H}^{s}_{x}})d\tau.\end{split}

(3.99)

Summing up our outcome (
3.67
), (
3.80
), (
3.85
), and (
3.99
), we have

\begin{split}E(t)\lesssim&E(0)+\|(\mathbf{v},h)\|^{5}_{H^{s}_{x}}+\|(\mathbf{v}_{0},h_{0})\|^{4}_{H^{s}_{x}}\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}(1+\|(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})(1+\|(\mathbf{v},h)\|^{3}_{{H}_{x}^{s}})E(\tau)d\tau\\
\lesssim&(1+E^{5}(0))\exp\left(5\int^{t}_{0}\|(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\right)\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}(1+\|(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})(1+\|(\mathbf{v},h)\|^{3}_{{H}_{x}^{s}})E(\tau)d\tau.\end{split}

(3.100)

Referring to (
3.100
) and applying Gronwall’s inequality along with (
3.68
) for
t\leq 1
, we obtain

\begin{split}E(t)\lesssim&\left\{(1+E^{5}(0))\exp(\int^{t}_{0}\|(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau)\right\}\\
&\times\exp\left(\int^{t}_{0}(1+\|(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})(1+\|(\mathbf{v},h)\|^{3}_{{H}_{x}^{s}})d\tau\right)\\
\lesssim&(1+E^{5}(0))\exp(5\int^{t}_{0}\|(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau)\\
&\times\exp\left(\big[1+E^{3}(0)\int^{t}_{0}\|(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\big]\cdot\int^{t}_{0}(1+\|(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})d\tau\right)\\
\lesssim&(1+E^{5}(0))\exp\left(5\big[1+E^{3}(0)\int^{t}_{0}\|(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\big]\cdot\big[\int^{t}_{0}\|(d\mathbf{v},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau+1\big]\right).\end{split}

Therefore, we complete the proof of Lemma
3.14
.
∎

Moreover, we also need the following energy estimates of some lower-order terms.

Lemma 3.15
.

Assume
s\in(\frac{7}{4},\frac{15}{8}]
. Let
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
be a solution of (
1.19
) on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Let
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathbf{Q}
be stated in (
1.20
)-(
1.21
). Then the following estimate holds:

\|\mathcal{D},\mathbf{Q}\|_{H_{x}^{s-1}}\lesssim\|dh,d\mathbf{v}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}.

(3.101)

Proof.

Seeing (
1.20
) and (
1.21
),
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathbf{Q}
are formulated by
(dh,d\mathbf{v})\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})
. By using Lemma
3.6
and the equation (
1.11
), we can get

\|\mathcal{D},\mathbf{Q}\|_{H_{x}^{s-1}}\lesssim\|dh,d\mathbf{v}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|dh,d\mathbf{v}\|_{H_{x}^{s-1}}\lesssim\|dh,d\mathbf{v}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}.

Therefore, the estimate (
3.101
) holds. We have finished the proof of this lemma.
∎

3.6.
Uniqueness of solutions

We now present two results regarding the uniqueness of the solution, which can be derived directly from theorem
3.14
.

Corollary 3.16
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq\frac{15}{8}
and (
1.23
) hold. Consider the Cauchy problem (
1.19
) with the initial data
(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0},

\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0})\in H^{s}\times H^{s}\times H^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}\times L^{8}
. If there exists a solution
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
for (
1.19
) and

(h,\mathbf{v})\in C([0,T],H_{x}^{s})
,
\mathbf{w}\in C([0,T],H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}})
,
\nabla\mathbf{w}\in C([0,T],L^{8}_{x})
and
(dh,d\mathbf{v})\in{L^{4}_{[0,T]}L^{\infty}_{x}}
, then it’s unique.

Corollary 3.17
.

Assume
s\in(\frac{7}{4},\frac{15}{8}]
and (
1.23
) hold. Consider the Cauchy problem (
1.19
) with the initial data
(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0},

\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0})\in H^{s}\times H^{s}\times H^{\frac{3}{2}}\times L^{8}
. If there exists a solution
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
for (
1.19
) and

(h,\mathbf{v})\in C([0,T^{*}],H_{x}^{s})
,
\mathbf{w}\in C([0,T^{*}],H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}})
,
\nabla\mathbf{w}\in C([0,T^{*}],L^{8}_{x})
and
(dh,d\mathbf{v})\in{L^{4}_{[0,T^{*}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}
, then it’s unique.

4.
Proof of Theorem
2.1

Due to Corollary
3.16
, it suffices to prove the existence and Strichartz estimates of solutions. We will reduce the proof to a conclusion for small, smooth, compactly supported data.

4.1.
Reduction to smooth initial data

We state a proposition as follows.

Proposition 4.1
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq\frac{15}{8}
and
\delta\in(0,s-\frac{7}{4})
. For each
M_{0}>0
, there exists
T,M_{1},M_{2}>0
(depending on
C_{0},c_{0},s,s_{0}
and
M_{0}
) such that, for each smooth initial data

(h_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
, which satisfies

\begin{split}&\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{L^{8}}\leq M_{0},\end{split}

(4.1)

there exists a smooth solution
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
to (
1.19
) on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
satisfying

\begin{split}&\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}L_{x}^{8}}\leq M_{1},\\
&\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}}\leq 1+C_{0}.\end{split}

(4.2)

Furthermore, the solution satisfies

(1)

dispersive estimate for
h
,
\mathbf{v}
and
\mathbf{v}_{+}

\|dh,d\mathbf{v}_{+},d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{4}_{[0,T]}C^{\delta}_{x}}\leq M_{2},

(4.3)

(2)

Let
f
satisfy the equation (
2.4
). For each
1\leq r\leq s+1
, the Cauchy problem (
2.4
) is well-posed in
H_{x}^{r}\times H_{x}^{r-1}
. Furthermore, the following energy estimate

\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H^{r}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq C_{M_{0}}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),

(4.4)

and Strichartz estimates

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C_{M_{0}}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),\ \ \ k<r-1,

(4.5)

hold. The same estimates hold with
\left<\nabla\right>^{k}
replaced by
\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d
. Above,
C_{M_{0}}
is also a constant depending on
C_{0},c_{0},s,s_{0}
and
M_{0}
.

Indeed, Theorem
2.1
is a consequence of Proposition
4.1
, which is presented as follows.

Proof of Theorem
2.1
by using Proposition
4.1
.

Let
\{(h_{0k},{\mathbf{v}}_{0k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}
be a sequence of smooth data converging to
(h_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0})
in
H^{s}
. Similarly, noting (
1.14
), we also define
\mathbf{w}_{0k}=(w^{0}_{0k},w^{1}_{0k},w^{2}_{0k})
by

w^{\alpha}_{0k}=\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}_{\ \ \ \gamma}\partial_{\beta}v^{\gamma}_{0k}.

Therefore,
\{(h_{0k},{\mathbf{v}}_{0k},\mathbf{w}_{0k},\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}
is a smooth sequence which converges to
(h_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0},\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0})
in
H^{s}\times H^{s}\times H^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}\times L^{8}
. Due to (
2.1
), for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, we have

\|h_{0k}\|_{H^{s}}+\|{\mathbf{v}}_{0k}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0k}\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0k}\|_{L^{8}}\leq 2M_{0}.

(4.6)

Due to (
4.6
), applying Proposition
4.1
, for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, there exists a solution
(h_{k},\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})
on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
to (
1.19
) with the initial data

(h_{k},\mathbf{v}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})|_{t=0}=(h_{0k},\mathbf{v}_{0k},\mathbf{w}_{0k}),

where
T
depends on
C_{0},c_{0},s,s_{0}
and
M_{0}
, and
\mathbf{w}_{k}=(w^{0}_{k},w^{1}_{k},w^{2}_{k})
satisfies

w^{\alpha}_{k}=\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}_{\ \ \ \gamma}\partial_{\beta}v^{\gamma}_{k}.

We also note that the solution of (
1.19
) also satisfies (
3.1
). We let
\mathbf{U}_{k}=(p_{k},v^{0}_{1k},v^{1}_{1k},v^{2}_{2k}),k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, where
p_{k}=p(h_{k})
. For every
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, we obtain

\begin{split}A^{0}(\mathbf{U}_{k})\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}_{k}+\textstyle{\sum^{2}_{i=1}}A^{i}(\mathbf{U}_{k})\partial_{i}\mathbf{U}_{k}=0.\end{split}

Therefore, for
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, we have

\begin{split}A^{0}(\mathbf{U}_{k})\partial_{t}(\mathbf{U}_{k}-\mathbf{U}_{l})+\textstyle{\sum^{2}_{i=1}}A^{i}(\mathbf{U}_{k})\partial_{i}(\mathbf{U}_{k}-\mathbf{U}_{l})=\left\{-A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U}_{k})+A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U}_{l})\right\}\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{U}_{l}.\end{split}

Then the energy estimate in
L_{x}^{2}
for the difference
\mathbf{U}_{k}-\mathbf{U}_{l}
gives

\frac{d}{dt}\|\mathbf{U}_{k}-\mathbf{U}_{l}\|^{2}_{L_{x}^{2}}\leq C_{k,l}\left(\|d\mathbf{U}_{k},d\mathbf{U}_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{U}_{k}-\mathbf{U}_{l}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{U}_{k}-\mathbf{U}_{l}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{U}_{l}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\right),

(4.7)

where
C_{k,l}
depends on the
L^{\infty}_{x}
norm of
\mathbf{U}_{k}
and
\mathbf{U}_{l}
. Using the Strichartz estimates (
4.3
), we get

\|d\mathbf{v}_{k},dh_{k}\|_{L^{4}_{[0,T]C^{\delta}_{x}}}\leq M_{1},\quad\forall k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}.

(4.8)

Due to (
4.2
), we can obtain

\|h_{k},\mathbf{v}_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}L_{x}^{8}}\leq M_{1}.

(4.9)

Integrating (
4.7
) from
0
to
t
and using (
4.8
) yields

\begin{split}\|(\mathbf{U}_{k}-\mathbf{U}_{l})(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}&\lesssim\|(\mathbf{U}_{k}-\mathbf{U}_{l})(0,\cdot)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim\|(h_{0k}-h_{0l},\mathbf{v}_{0k}-\mathbf{v}_{0l})\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

By using Lemma
3.8
, it follows

\begin{split}\|(h_{k}-h_{l},\mathbf{v}_{k}-\mathbf{v}_{l})(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}&\lesssim\|(h_{0k}-h_{0l},\mathbf{v}_{0k}-\mathbf{v}_{0l})\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

Therefore,
\{(h_{k},\mathbf{v}_{k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
C([0,T];L_{x}^{2})
. Denote
(p,u_{1},u_{2})
being the limit. Then
(h,\mathbf{v})\in C([0,T];L_{x}^{2})
and

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}(h_{k},\mathbf{v}_{k})=(h,\mathbf{v})\ \mathrm{in}\ C([0,T];L_{x}^{2}).

Using (
4.9
), the function
(h,\mathbf{v})\in C([0,T];H_{x}^{s})
. Moreover, we also get

\|(h,\mathbf{v})\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}L_{x}^{8}}\leq M_{1},

This implies that the estimate (
2.2
) holds. By using interpolation formula and (
4.1
), we conclude

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}(h_{k},\mathbf{v}_{k})=(h,\mathbf{v})\ \ \mathrm{in}\ \ C([0,T];H_{x}^{s^{\prime}}),\quad s^{\prime}<s.

(4.10)

Applying (
1.19
),
\mathbf{w}_{j}
satisfies the following transport equation:

\begin{split}v^{\kappa}_{j}\partial_{\kappa}w^{\alpha}_{j}=w^{\kappa}_{j}\partial^{\alpha}v_{\kappa j}-w^{\alpha}_{j}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\kappa}_{j}.\end{split}

By
L^{2}_{x}
energy estimates for the difference term
\mathbf{w}_{j}-\mathbf{w}_{l}
, we have

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\|\mathbf{w}_{j}-\mathbf{w}_{l}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{v}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}_{j}-\mathbf{w}_{l}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{v}_{j}-\mathbf{v}_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}_{j}-\mathbf{w}_{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&+\|\mathbf{w}_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{v}_{j}-d\mathbf{v}_{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}_{j}-\mathbf{w}_{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{v}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}_{j}-\mathbf{w}_{l}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{v}_{k}-\mathbf{v}_{l}\|_{H^{1+a}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}_{l}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}_{j}-\mathbf{w}_{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&+\|\mathbf{w}_{l}\|_{H^{1+a}_{x}}\|\mathbf{v}_{k}-\mathbf{v}_{l}\|_{H^{1}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}_{j}-\mathbf{w}_{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}},\end{split}

for
a>0
. Integrating (
4.1
) from
0
to
t
, using the Gronwall’s inequality, product estimates and Proposition
4.1
, we obtain

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}_{l}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{v}_{0k}-\mathbf{v}_{0l}\|_{H^{\frac{7}{4}}}+\|h_{0k}-h_{0l}\|_{H^{\frac{7}{4}}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0k}-\mathbf{w}_{0l}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

As a result,
\{\mathbf{w}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
is a Cauchy sequence in the space
L_{x}^{2}
. Let
\mathbf{w}
denote its limit. Since
\mathbf{w}_{k}
is also bounded in
C([0,T];H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}})
, it follows that
\mathbf{w}\in C([0,T];H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}})
.

It remains for us to prove (
2.3
) and (
2.5
). Due to Proposition
4.1
again, the sequence
\{(d\mathbf{v}_{k},dh_{k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{4}([0,T];C_{x}^{\delta})
. Consequently, we have

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}(d\mathbf{v}_{k},dh_{k})=(d\mathbf{v},dh),\quad\textrm{in}\ L^{4}([0,T];L_{x}^{\infty}).

(4.11)

Similarly, we also get

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}d\mathbf{v}_{+k}=d\mathbf{v}_{+},\quad\textrm{in}\ L^{4}([0,T];L_{x}^{\infty}).

(4.12)

Combining (
4.8
) and (
4.11
), (
4.12
), we get

\|(d\mathbf{v},dh,d\mathbf{v}_{+})\|_{L^{4}_{[0,T]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq M_{1}.

This implies the estimate (
2.3
) holds. Take
T\leq\frac{1}{(1+M_{1})^{\frac{4}{3}}}
. We therefore obtain

\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C_{0}+T^{\frac{3}{4}}\|d\mathbf{v},dh\|_{L^{4}_{[0,T]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq 1+C_{0}.

Using (
4.4
) and (
4.5
), we have

\|f_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H^{r}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}f_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq C_{M_{0}}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),

and Strichartz estimates

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{a}f_{k}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C_{M_{0}}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),\ \ \ a<r-1,

Taking
k\rightarrow\infty
and setting
f_{k}\rightarrow f
in
H^{r}_{x}
, the estimate (
2.5
) holds. Therefore, we complete the proof of Theorem
2.1
.
∎

It remains for us to verify Proposition
4.1
.

4.2.
Reduction to small, smooth, and compactly supported data

Note that the propagation speed of (
1.19
) is finite. We therefore set the constant
c>0
being the largest speed of
propagation corresponding to the acoustic metric. We can further reduce Proposition
4.1
to this following
result:

Proposition 4.2
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq\frac{15}{8}
and
\delta\in(0,s-\frac{7}{4})
. Suppose the initial data
(h_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
be smooth, supported in
B(0,c+2)
and satisfy

\begin{split}&\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{L^{8}}\leq\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(4.13)

Then the Cauchy problem of (
1.19
) admits a smooth solution
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Moreover, it has the following properties:

(1)

energy estimate

\begin{split}&\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}L_{x}^{8}}\leq\epsilon_{2},\\
&\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}}\leq 1+C_{0}.\end{split}

(4.14)

(2)

dispersive estimate for
h
and
\mathbf{v}

\|d\mathbf{v},dh,d\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{4}_{[-2,2]}C^{\delta}_{x}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(4.15)

where
\mathbf{v}_{+}
is defined as in (
1.16
)-(
1.18
).

(3)

dispersive estimate for the linear equation

Let
f
satisfy

\begin{cases}&\square_{g}f=0,\qquad(t,x)\in[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2},\\
&(f,\partial_{t}f)|_{t=t_{0}}=(f_{0},f_{1})\in H_{x}^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H_{x}^{r-1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),\end{cases}

(4.16)

where
g
is defined in (
1.13
). For each
1\leq r\leq s+1
, the Cauchy problem (
4.16
) has a unique solution in
C([-2,2];H_{x}^{r})\cap C^{1}([-2,2];H_{x}^{r-1})
. Furthermore, for
k<r-1
, we also have

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}},

(4.17)

and the same estimates hold with
\left<\nabla\right>^{k}
replaced by
\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d
.

Assuming Proposition
4.2
holds, we can prove Proposition
4.1
as follows.

Proof of Proposition
4.1
by using Proposition
4.2
.

We divide this proof into three steps.

Step 1: Scaling
. We note the initial data in Proposition
4.1
satisfying

\begin{split}&\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{L^{8}}\leq M_{0},\end{split}

(4.18)

which is not small. Take the scaling

\begin{split}&\underline{h}(t,x)=h(Tt,Tx),\quad\underline{{\mathbf{v}}}(t,x)=\mathbf{v}(Tt,Tx).\end{split}

Due to (
4.18
), we can compute out

\begin{split}&\|\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}=T^{s-1}\|{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq M_{0}T^{s-1}.\end{split}

(4.19)

Similarly, we obtain

\begin{split}&\|\underline{h}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq M_{0}T^{s-1}.\end{split}

(4.20)

Also, we can calculate

\begin{split}\|\underline{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}=&\|\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\underline{v}_{0\gamma}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}\\
=&T^{s_{0}+\frac{3}{4}}\|\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}{v}_{0\gamma}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}\\
=&T^{s_{0}+\frac{3}{4}}\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{L^{8}}\\
\leq&M_{0}T^{s_{0}+\frac{3}{4}},\end{split}

(4.21)

and

\begin{split}\|\nabla\underline{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{L^{8}}=&\|\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\underline{v}_{0\gamma}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,8}}\\
=&T^{\frac{7}{4}}\|\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}{v}_{0\gamma}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,8}}\\
=&T^{\frac{7}{4}}\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{\dot{W}^{1,8}}\\
\leq&M_{0}T^{\frac{7}{4}}.\end{split}

(4.22)

Choose sufficiently small
T
such that

\max\{M_{0}T^{s-1},M_{0}T^{s_{0}+\frac{3}{4}},M_{0}T^{\frac{7}{4}}\}\ll\epsilon_{3}.

(4.23)

Therefore, by (
4.19
), (
4.20
), (
4.21
), and (
4.23
), we get

\begin{split}&\|\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}+\|\underline{h}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}+\|\underline{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\underline{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{L^{8}}\leq\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(4.24)

Using (
1.23
) and (
1.1
), we also have

\|\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0},\underline{h}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C_{0}.

(4.25)

Next, to reduce the initial data with compact support, we use the physical localization technique.

Step 2: Localization
. Note that the propagation speed of (
1.19
) is finite and
c
be the largest one. Let
\chi(x)
be a smooth function supported in
B(0,c+2)
, and which equals
1
in
B(0,c+1)
. Define

\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}=(\underline{v}^{0}_{0},\mathring{\underline{\mathbf{v}}}_{0}).

For given
y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we define the localized initial data for the velocity and density near
y
:

\begin{split}\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}}_{0}(x)=&\chi(x-y)(\underline{\mathring{\mathbf{v}}}_{0}(x)-\mathring{\underline{\mathbf{v}}}_{0}(y)),\\
\widetilde{h}_{0}(x)=&\chi(x-y)(\underline{h}_{0}(x)-\underline{h}_{0}(y)).\end{split}

(4.26)

Since the velocity
\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}
satisfies (
1.1
), so we set

{\widetilde{v}^{0}}_{0}=\sqrt{\mathrm{e}^{2\widetilde{h}_{0}}+|\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}}_{0}|^{2}},\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0}=({\widetilde{v}^{0}}_{0},\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}}_{0}).

(4.27)

By using (
4.25
), (
4.26
), and (
4.27
), we have

\|\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}}_{0},\widetilde{h}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C_{0},\quad 1\leq{\widetilde{v}^{0}}_{0}\leq 1+C_{0}.

(4.28)

Due to (
4.26
) and (
4.27
), we can verify that

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{{H}^{s}}\lesssim\|\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}},\quad\|\widetilde{h}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\|\underline{h}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}.

(4.29)

By use of (
1.14
), we define

\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}=\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{v}_{0\gamma}.

(4.30)

For
\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0}
is the initial data, combining (
4.30
) and (
4.28
) yield

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{\mathbf{w}}}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}.

(4.31)

Due to (
4.30
) and (
4.26
), we infer

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\|\underline{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}},\quad\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{L^{8}}\lesssim\|\nabla\underline{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{L^{8}}.\end{split}

(4.32)

Combining (
4.29
), (
4.24
), (
4.31
), and (
4.32
), we therefore conclude

\begin{split}&\|\widetilde{h}_{0}\|_{{H}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{{H}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{{H}^{s_{0}}}+\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{{L}^{8}}\lesssim\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(4.33)

Step 3: Using Proposition
4.2
.
Under the initial conditions (
4.28
) and (
4.33
), by Proposition
4.2
, there is a smooth solution
(\widetilde{h},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}})
on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that

\begin{cases}\square_{\widetilde{g}}\widetilde{h}=\widetilde{\mathcal{D}},\\
\square_{\widetilde{g}}\widetilde{v}^{\alpha}=-\widetilde{c}^{2}_{s}\widetilde{\Theta}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}+\widetilde{Q}^{\alpha},\\
(\widetilde{v}^{\kappa}+\underline{v}^{\kappa}_{0}(y))\partial_{\kappa}\widetilde{w}^{\alpha}=\widetilde{w}^{\kappa}\partial^{\alpha}\widetilde{v}_{\kappa}-\widetilde{w}^{\alpha}\partial_{\kappa}\widetilde{v}^{\kappa},\\
(\widetilde{h},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}})|_{t=0}=(\widetilde{h}_{0},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}),\end{cases}

(4.34)

where the quantities
\widetilde{c}^{2}_{s}
,
\widetilde{{g}}
, and
\widetilde{\Theta}
are defined by

\begin{split}\widetilde{c}^{2}_{s}&=c^{2}_{s}(\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y)),\\
\widetilde{g}&=g(\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y),\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}+\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(y)),\\
\widetilde{\Theta}&={\Theta}(\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y),\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}+\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(y)),\end{split}

and
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}},\widetilde{Q}^{\alpha}
have the same formulations with
\mathcal{D}
,
{Q}^{\alpha}
by replacing
({h},\mathbf{v})
to
(\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y),\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}+\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}(y))
.

Next, we consider a better component of the velocity
\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}
. We define

\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}=\mathrm{I}-\left[m^{\alpha\beta}+2\mathrm{e}^{2(\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y))}(\widetilde{v}^{\alpha}+\underline{v}^{\alpha}_{0}(y))(\widetilde{v}^{\beta}+\underline{v}^{\beta}_{0}(y))\right]\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}.

(4.35)

Referring to (
4.35
) and using Lemma
3.11
, we conclude that
\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}
is a space-time elliptic operator. Define
two vectors
\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{-}=(\widetilde{v}^{0}_{-},\widetilde{v}^{1}_{-},\widetilde{v}^{2}_{-})
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{+}=(\widetilde{v}^{0}_{+},\widetilde{v}^{1}_{+},\widetilde{v}^{2}_{+})
by

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\widetilde{v}^{\alpha}_{-}&=\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{w}_{\gamma},\\
\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{+}&=\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}-\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{-}.\end{split}

Denote an operator

\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}=\partial_{t}+\frac{\widetilde{v}^{i}+\underline{v}^{i}_{0}(y)}{\widetilde{v}^{0}+\underline{v}^{0}_{0}(y)}\partial_{i}.

Due to (
3.23
), we have

\begin{split}\square_{g}\widetilde{v}^{\alpha}_{+}&=\widetilde{\Theta}(1-3\widetilde{c}^{2}_{s})\mathrm{e}^{-2(\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y))}(\widetilde{v}^{\beta}+\underline{\widetilde{v}}^{\beta}_{0}(y))(\widetilde{v}^{\gamma}+\underline{\widetilde{v}}^{\gamma}_{0}(y))\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}\widetilde{v}^{\alpha}_{-}-\widetilde{c}^{2}_{s}\widetilde{\Theta}\widetilde{v}^{\alpha}_{-}+\widetilde{Q}^{\alpha}.\end{split}

Using Proposition
4.2
again, the solution
(\widetilde{h},\widetilde{{\mathbf{v}}},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}})
also satisfies

\|\widetilde{h},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}L_{x}^{8}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(4.36)

and

\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},d\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{4}_{[-2,2]}C^{\delta}_{x}}\leq\epsilon_{2}.

Moreover, the linear wave equation endowed with the Lorentzian metric
\widetilde{{g}}

\begin{cases}&\square_{\widetilde{{g}}}\tilde{f}=0,\\
&\tilde{f}(t_{0},\cdot)=\widetilde{f}_{0},\ \partial_{t}\tilde{f}(t_{0},)=\widetilde{f}_{1},\end{cases}

also admits a solution
\widetilde{f}\in C([-2,2],H_{x}^{r})\times C^{1}([-2,2],H_{x}^{r-1})
. Furthermore, if
k<r-1
, the following Strichartz estimate holds:

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k}\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|\widetilde{f}_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|\widetilde{f}_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}.

On the other hand, set

\begin{split}\bar{h}(t,x)&=\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y),\\
\bar{{\mathbf{v}}}(t,x)&=(\bar{v}^{0},\mathring{\bar{\mathbf{v}}}),\quad\bar{v}^{0}=\sqrt{\mathrm{e}^{2\bar{h}}+|\mathring{\bar{\mathbf{v}}}|^{2}},\\
\mathring{\bar{\mathbf{v}}}(t,x)&=\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}}+\underline{\mathring{\mathbf{v}}}_{0}(y),\\
\bar{w}^{\alpha}(t,x)&=\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\bar{{v}}_{\gamma}.\end{split}

(4.37)

Therefore,
(\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{v}},\bar{\mathbf{w}})
is also a solution of (
4.34
), and its initial data coincides with
(h_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
in
B(y,c+1)
. Consider a restriction

(\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{v}},\bar{\mathbf{w}})|_{\mathrm{K}^{y}},

(4.38)

where
y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\mathrm{K}^{y}=\left\{(t,x):ct+|x-y|\leq c+1,|t|<1\right\}
. Then (
4.38
) solves (
4.34
) on
\mathrm{K}^{y}
. By finite speed of propagation, a smooth solution
(\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{v}},\bar{\mathbf{w}})
solves (
1.19
) on
[-1,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Without diffusion, we still use the notation
\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{v}}
and
\bar{\mathbf{w}}
being the restrictions on
\mathrm{K}^{y}
.

From (
4.37
), by time-space scaling
(t,x)
to
(T^{-1}t,T^{-1}x)
, we also obtain

\begin{split}&(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})=(\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{v}},\bar{\mathbf{w}})(T^{-1}t,T^{-1}x),\\
&(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})|_{t=0}=(\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{v}},\bar{\mathbf{w}})(0,T^{-1}x)=(h_{0},\mathbf{u}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0}).\end{split}

(4.39)

Therefore, the function
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
defined in (
4.39
) is the solution of (
1.19
) according to the uniqueness of solutions, i.e. Corollary
3.16
. To obtain the energy estimates for
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we use the cartesian grid
3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{2}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and a corresponding smooth partition of unity

\textstyle{\sum}_{y\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\psi(x-y)=1,

such that the function
\psi
is supported in the unit ball. Therefore, we have

\begin{split}&\mathring{\bar{\mathbf{v}}}=\textstyle{\sum}_{y\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\psi(x-y)(\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}}+\underline{\mathring{\mathbf{v}}}_{0}(y)),\\
&\bar{h}=\textstyle{\sum}_{y\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\psi(x-y)(\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y)),\\
&\bar{v}^{0}=\sqrt{\mathrm{e}^{2\bar{h}}+|\mathring{\bar{\mathbf{v}}}|^{2}},\quad\bar{\mathbf{v}}=(\bar{v}^{0},\mathring{\bar{\mathbf{v}}}),\\
&\bar{w}^{\alpha}=\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\bar{v}_{\gamma}.\end{split}

(4.40)

Due to (
4.36
), (
4.25
), and (
4.40
), we shall obtain

\begin{split}&\|\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{v}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq\|\widetilde{h},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\underline{h}_{0},\underline{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq 1+C_{0}.\end{split}

(4.41)

By changing the coordinates, it yields

\begin{split}&\|d{\mathbf{v}},d{h}\|_{L^{4}_{[0,T]}C^{\delta}_{x}}\\
\leq&T^{-(\frac{3}{4}+{\delta})}\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{v}},d\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{4}_{[0,1]}C^{\delta}_{x}}\\
\leq&CT^{-(\frac{3}{4}+{\delta})}(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{h}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{8}})\\
\leq&CT^{-(\frac{3}{4}+{\delta})}(T^{s-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s}}+T^{s-1}\|h_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s}}+T^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}\|{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+T^{\frac{7}{4}}\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{L^{8}_{x}})\\
\leq&C(\|{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|{h}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}).\end{split}

(4.42)

Above, we use
{\delta}\in(0,s-\frac{7}{4})
and
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq 2
. By using (
4.41
) and changing of coordinates from
(t,x)\rightarrow(T^{-1}t,T^{-1}x)
, we can obtain

\begin{split}&\|{\mathbf{v}},{h}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}}=\|{\bar{\mathbf{v}}},\bar{h}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq 1+C_{0},\end{split}

(4.43)

and

\begin{split}&v^{0}=\sqrt{\mathrm{e}^{2h}+|\mathring{\mathbf{v}}|^{2}}\geq\mathrm{e}^{h}.\end{split}

(4.44)

Applying Theorem
3.14
along with equations (
4.42
), (
4.43
), and (
4.44
), we conclude that
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
satisfies (
4.1
), (
4.2
) and (
4.3
).

It remains for us to prove (
4.4
) and (
4.5
). For
1\leq r\leq s+1
, we consider the following homogeneous linear wave equation

\begin{cases}\square_{{g}}f=0,\qquad[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{2},\\
(f,f_{t})|_{t=0}=(f_{0},f_{1})\in H_{x}^{r}\times H^{r-1}_{x}.\end{cases}

(4.45)

For (
4.45
) is a scaling invariant system, we can transfer (
4.45
) to the localized linear equation

\begin{cases}\square_{\widetilde{g}}{f}^{y}=0,\quad[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2},\\
({f}^{y},{f}^{y}_{t})|_{t=0}=(f_{0}^{y},f^{y}_{1}),\end{cases}

(4.46)

where

\begin{split}&f_{0}^{y}=\chi(x-y)(\widetilde{f}_{0}-\widetilde{f}_{0}(y)),\quad f^{y}_{1}=\chi(x-y)\widetilde{f}_{1},\\
&\widetilde{f}_{0}=f_{0}(Tx),\quad\widetilde{f}_{1}=f_{1}(Tx).\end{split}

Let

\widetilde{f}=\textstyle{\sum}_{y\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\psi(x-y)({f}^{y}+\widetilde{f}_{0}(y)).

(4.47)

Seeing (
4.46
), and using Proposition
4.2
again, for
k<r-1
, we get

\begin{split}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d{f}^{y}\|_{L^{4}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(\|\chi(x-y)(\widetilde{f}_{0}-\widetilde{f}_{0}(y))\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|\chi(x-y)\widetilde{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}})\\
\leq&C(\|\widetilde{f}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|\widetilde{f}_{1}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.48)

Due to the finite speed of propagation, we can claim
\widetilde{f}={f}^{y}+\widetilde{f}_{0}(y)
in
\mathrm{K}^{y}
. Applying (
4.47
) and (
4.48
), we obtain

\begin{split}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{4}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}=&\sup_{y\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d({f}^{y}+\widetilde{f}_{0}(y))\|_{L^{4}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
=&\sup_{y\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d{f}^{y}\|_{L^{4}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(\|\widetilde{f}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|\widetilde{f}_{1}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.49)

By a change of coordinates:
(t,x)\rightarrow(T^{-1}t,T^{-1}x)
, we have

\begin{split}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d{f}\|_{L^{4}_{[0,T]}L^{\infty}_{x}}=&T^{k-\frac{1}{4}}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{4}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}.\end{split}

(4.50)

Taking
T\leq 1
, combing (
4.49
) with (
4.50
), if
k<r-1
, then we have

\begin{split}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d{f}\|_{L^{4}_{[0,T]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&CT^{r-1-k}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r-1}})\\
\leq&C(\|{f}_{0}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.51)

Using the energy estimates for (
4.45
) and (
4.42
), we can carry out

\begin{split}\|d{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq&C_{M_{0}}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}})\int^{T}_{0}\|d\mathbf{v},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\\
\leq&C_{M_{0}}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.52)

Due to Newton-Leibniz formula
f=\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{t}fd\tau-f_{0}
, we therefore get

\begin{split}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}L^{2}_{x}}\leq&T\|d{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}L^{2}_{x}}+\|{f}_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
\leq&C_{M_{0}}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.53)

Adding (
4.52
) and (
4.53
), we therefore obtain

\begin{split}\|{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H^{r}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq C_{M_{0}}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.54)

Because of (
4.51
), (
4.52
), (
4.53
), and (
4.54
), the estimates (
4.4
) and (
4.5
) hold. Thus, we complete the proof of Proposition
4.1
.
∎

It remains for us to verify Proposition
4.2
.

5.
Proof of Proposition
4.2

In this section, we will establish Proposition
4.2
through a bootstrap argument. For technical reasons, it is convenient for us to replace the original acoustic metric by a truncated one. To do this, we define two vectors

\mathbf{0}=(0,0,0)^{\mathrm{T}},\quad\mathbf{1}=(1,0,0)^{\mathrm{T}}.

We note that
h=0,\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{1},\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{0}
is an equilibrium state for the system (
1.19
). Then the initial data in (
4.13
) can be seen as a small perturbation around this equilibrium state. By setting
h=0
and
\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{1}
in
g
(see (
1.13
)), we denote this as
g_{*}
. The inverse matrix of the metric
g_{*}
is

g^{-1}_{*}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}-1&0&0\\
0&c^{2}_{s}(0)&0\\
0&0&c^{2}_{s}(0)\end{array}\right),

where
c_{s}(0)=c_{s}(h)|_{h=0}
. By a linear change of coordinates which preserves
dt
, we may assume that
g^{\alpha\beta}_{*}=m^{\alpha\beta}
. Let
\chi
be a smooth cut-off function supported in the region
B(0,3+2c)\times[-\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2}]
, which equals to
1
in the region
B(0,2+2c)\times[-1,1]
. Define
\mathbf{g}=(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta})_{3\times 3}
and

\begin{split}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}=&\chi(t,x)(g^{\alpha\beta}-g^{\alpha\beta}_{*})+g^{\alpha\beta}_{*},\end{split}

(5.1)

where
g^{\alpha\beta}=\Theta\{c_{s}^{2}m^{\alpha\beta}+\mathrm{e}^{-2h}(c_{s}^{2}-1)v^{\alpha}v^{\beta}\}
is stated as in (
1.13
). Moreover, using (
5.1
), we have

\mathbf{g}=g,\quad\textrm{if}\ (t,x)\in[-1,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}.

Therefore,
\mathbf{g}
is an extension of the metric
g
. Consider the following wave-transport system:

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}h=\mathcal{D},\\
\square_{\mathbf{g}}v^{\alpha}=-c^{2}_{s}\Theta\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}+Q^{\alpha},\\
v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w^{\alpha}=w^{\kappa}\partial^{\alpha}v_{\kappa}-w^{\alpha}\partial_{\kappa}v^{\kappa},\end{cases}

(5.2)

where
\mathcal{D}
and
Q^{\alpha}
(
\alpha=0,1,2
) have the same formulations as in equations (
1.20
) and (
1.21
). Consequently, the system (
5.2
) is an extension of (
1.19
) from
[-1,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
to
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Definition 5.1
.

(Definition for
\mathcal{H}
)
Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq\frac{15}{8}
and
\delta\in(0,s-\frac{7}{4})
. We denote by
\mathcal{H}
the family of smooth solutions
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
to the equation (
5.2
) for
t\in[-2,2]
, with the initial data
(h_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
supported in
B(0,2+c)
, and for which

\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{L^{8}}\leq\epsilon_{3},

(5.3)

\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}L_{x}^{8}}\leq 2\epsilon_{2},

(5.4)

\|d\mathbf{v},dh,d\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{4}_{[-2,2]}C_{x}^{\delta}}\leq 2\epsilon_{2}.

(5.5)

The bootstrap argument can be stated as follows.

5.1.
A bootstrap argument

Proposition 5.1
.

Assume
s\in(\frac{7}{4},\frac{15}{8}]
and that (
1.24
) holds. Then there exists a continuous functional
\Re:\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
, satisfying
\Re(0,\mathbf{1},\mathbf{0})=0
, such that for each
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
satisfying
\Re(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
the following hold:

(1)

The function
h
,
\mathbf{v}
, and
\mathbf{w}
satisfies

\Re(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\leq\epsilon_{1}.

(5.6)

(2)

The energy estimate holds:

\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}L_{x}^{8}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(5.7)

and the Strichartz estimate

\|dh,d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{4}_{[-2,2]}C^{\delta}_{x}}\leq\epsilon_{2}.

(5.8)

(3)

For any
1\leq k\leq s+1
, and for each
t_{0}\in[-2,2)
, the linear wave equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=0,\qquad(t,x)\in[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2},\\
&(f,\partial_{t}f)|_{t=t_{0}}=(f_{0},f_{1})\in H^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\times H^{r-1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),\end{cases}

admits a unique solution
f\in C([-2,2],H_{x}^{r})\times C^{1}([-2,2],H_{x}^{r-1})
. Furthermore, the Strichartz estimate (
4.17
) holds.

Based on Proposition
5.1
, we are ready to prove Proposition
4.2
.

Proof of Proposition
4.2
by using Proposition
5.1
.

Consider the initial data in Proposition
4.2
satisfying

\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{L^{8}}\leq\epsilon_{3}.

Denote by
\mathcal{A}
the subset of those
\gamma\in[0,1]
such that the equation (
5.2
) admits a smooth solution
(h_{\gamma},\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})
having the initial data

\begin{split}h_{\gamma}(0)=&\gamma h_{0},\\
\mathring{\mathbf{v}}_{\gamma}(0)=&\gamma\mathring{\mathbf{v}}_{0},\\
v^{0}_{\gamma}(0)=&\sqrt{\mathrm{e}^{2h_{\gamma}(0)}+|\mathring{\mathbf{v}}_{\gamma}(0)|^{2}},\\
w^{\alpha}_{\gamma}(0)=&\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}_{\ \ \ \kappa}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}_{0\gamma},\end{split}

and such that
\Re(h_{\gamma},\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})\leq\epsilon_{1}
and (
5.7
), (
5.8
) hold. If
\gamma=0
, then

(h_{\gamma},{\mathbf{v}}_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})(t,x)=(0,\mathbf{1},\mathbf{0}),

is a smooth solution of (
5.2
) with initial data

(h_{\gamma},{\mathbf{v}}_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})(0,x)=(0,\mathbf{1},\mathbf{0}).

Thus, the set
\mathcal{A}
is not empty. If we can prove that
\mathcal{A}=[0,1]
, thus
1\in\mathcal{A}
. Also, (
4.13
), (
4.14
), (
4.15
), (
8.1
), and (
4.17
) follow from Proposition
5.1
. As a result, Proposition
4.2
holds. It is sufficient to prove that
\mathcal{A}
is both open and closed in
[0,1]
.

(1)
\mathcal{A}
is open. Let
\gamma\in\mathcal{A}
. Then
(h_{\gamma},\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})
is a smooth solution to (
5.2
), where

w^{\alpha}_{\gamma}=\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}_{\ \ \ \kappa}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}_{\gamma}.

Let
\beta
be close to
\gamma
. Since
(h^{\gamma},\mathbf{v}^{\gamma},\mathbf{w}^{\gamma})
is smooth, a perturbation argument
shows that the equation (
5.2
) has a smooth solution
(h^{\beta},\mathbf{v}^{\beta},\mathbf{w}^{\beta})
, which
depends continuously on
\beta
. By the continuity of
\Re
, for
\beta
close to
\gamma
, we infer

\Re(h_{\beta},\mathbf{v}_{\beta},\mathbf{w}_{\beta})\leq 2\epsilon_{1},

and (
5.3
), (
5.4
), (
5.5
) hold. Due to Proposition
5.1
, we get

\Re(h_{\beta},\mathbf{v}_{\beta},\mathbf{w}_{\beta})\leq\epsilon_{1},

and also (
5.7
)-(
5.8
) hold. Thus, we have showed that
\beta\in\mathcal{A}
.

(2)
\mathcal{A}
is closed. Let
\gamma_{k}\in\mathcal{A},k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
. Let
\gamma
satisfy
\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\gamma_{k}=\gamma
.
Then there exists a sequence
\{(h_{\gamma_{k}},\mathbf{v}_{\gamma_{k}},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma_{k}})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
being the smooth solutions to (
5.2
) and

\displaystyle\|h_{\gamma_{k}},\mathbf{v}_{\gamma_{k}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{\gamma_{k}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{\gamma_{k}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}L_{x}^{8}}+\|dh_{\gamma_{k}},d\mathbf{v}_{\gamma_{k}}\|_{L^{4}_{[-2,2]}C_{x}^{\delta}}\leq\epsilon_{2}.

Then there exists a subsequence
\{(h_{\gamma_{k}},\mathbf{v}_{\gamma_{k}},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma_{k}})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
such that

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}(h_{\gamma_{k}},\mathbf{v}_{\gamma_{k}},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma_{k}})=(h_{\gamma},\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma}).

Moreover,
(h_{\gamma},\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})
satisfies

\begin{split}&\|h_{\gamma},\mathbf{v}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}L_{x}^{8}}+\|dh_{\gamma},d\mathbf{v}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{4}_{[-2,2]}C_{x}^{\delta}}\leq\epsilon_{2},\end{split}

Thus,
\Re(h_{\gamma},\mathbf{v}_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})\leq\epsilon_{1}
. Then
\gamma\in\mathcal{A}
. At this stage, we have proved Proposition
4.2
.
∎

Note that there is no precise definition of the functional
\Re
. We now give the definition.

5.2.
Definition for the functional

Let us first define foliations for the space-time and introduce new norms.

Assume
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
. Give a extension for
\mathbf{g}
(
\mathbf{g}
is defined in (
5.1
)), which equals to the Minkowski metric for
t\in[-2,-\frac{3}{2}]
. When no confusion can arise, we still use the notation
\mathbf{g}
.

For each
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
, we consider a foliation of the slice
t=-2
by taking level sets of the function
r_{\theta}(-2,x)=\theta\cdot x+2
. Then
\theta\cdot dx-t
is a null covector field over
t=-2
, and it is co-normal to the level sets of
r_{\theta}(-2)
. Let
\Gamma_{\theta}
be the graph of a null covector field given by
dr_{\theta}
, We define the hypersurface
\Sigma_{\theta,r}
for
r\in\mathbb{R}
as the level sets of
r_{\theta}
. The characteristic hypersurface
\Sigma_{\theta,r}
is thus the flowout of the set
\theta\cdot x=r-2
along the null geodesic flow in the direction
\theta
at
t=-2
.

We introduce an orthonormal sets of coordinates on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
by setting
x_{\theta}=\theta\cdot x
. Let
x^{\prime}_{\theta}
be given orthonormal coordinates on the hyperplane prependicular to
\theta
, which then define coordinates on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
by projection along
\theta
. Then
(t,x^{\prime}_{\theta})
induce the coordinates on
\Sigma_{\theta,r}
, and
\Sigma_{\theta,r}
is given by

\Sigma_{\theta,r}=\left\{(t,x):x_{\theta}-\phi_{\theta,r}=0\right\},

for a smooth function
\phi_{\theta,r}(t,x^{\prime}_{\theta})
. We now introduce two norms for functions defined on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, for
a\geq 1
,

\begin{split}&|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{a,\infty}=\sup_{-2\leq t\leq 2}\sup_{0\leq j\leq 1}\|\partial_{t}^{j}f(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{a-j}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\\
&|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{a,2}=\big(\sup_{0\leq j\leq 1}\int^{2}_{-2}\|\partial_{t}^{j}f(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{H^{a-j}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}dt\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

The same notation applies for functions in
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
. We denote

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{a,2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}=|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|_{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{a,2},

where the right hand side is the norm of the restriction of
f
to
{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}
, taken over the
(t,x^{\prime}_{\theta})
variables used to parametrise
{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}
. Similarly, the notation

\|f\|_{H^{a}(\Sigma_{\theta,r})},

denotes the
H^{a}(\mathbb{R})
norm of
f
restricted to the time
t
slice of
{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}
using the
x^{\prime}_{\theta}
coordinates on
{\Sigma^{t}_{\theta,r}}
.

For
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}<s\leq\frac{15}{8}
, we define the functional
\Re
by

\Re=\sup_{\theta,r}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi_{\theta,r}-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}}.

(5.9)

5.3.
The proof of Proposition
5.1

We postpone the proof and proceed to state the following two propositions.

Proposition 5.2
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq\frac{15}{8}
and
\delta_{0}\in(0,s_{0}-\frac{7}{4})
. Let
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
such that
\Re(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. Then

\Re(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(5.10)

Furthermore, for each
t
it holds that

\|d\phi_{\theta,r}(t,\cdot)-dt\|_{C^{1,\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}+\|d{\mathbf{g}}(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{\delta_{0}}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

(5.11)

Proposition 5.3
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq\frac{15}{8}
. Suppose that
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{{H}}
and
\Re(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
.
For any
1\leq r\leq s+1
, and for each
t_{0}\in[-2,2]
, the linear, non-homogenous equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=F,\qquad(t,x)\in[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2},\\
&f(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{0}\in H_{x}^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),\\
&\partial_{t}f(t,x)|_{t=t_{0}}=f_{1}\in H_{x}^{r-1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),\end{cases}

admits a solution
f\in C([-2,2],H_{x}^{r})\times C^{1}([-2,2],H_{x}^{r-1})
and the following estimates holds:

\|f\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{x}^{r}}+\|\partial_{t}f\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{x}^{r-1}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}+\|F\|_{L^{1}_{t}H_{x}^{r-1}}.

(5.12)

Additionally, the following estimates hold, provided
a<r-\frac{3}{4}
,

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{a}f\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}+\|F\|_{L^{1}_{t}H_{x}^{r-1}}.

(5.13)

The similar estimate (
5.13
) holds if we replace
\left<\nabla\right>^{a}
by
\left<\nabla\right>^{a-1}d
.

Based on Propositions
5.2
and
5.3
, we are now ready to present the proof of Proposition
5.1
.

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

Due to Proposition
5.2
and Proposition
5.3
, it remains for us to verify (
5.7
) and (
5.8
). Applying Theorem
3.14
, (
5.3
), and (
5.5
), we have

\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}L_{x}^{8}}\lesssim\epsilon_{3}.

(5.14)

Note that
\epsilon_{3}\ll\epsilon_{2}
. Therefore, the energy estimate (
5.7
) holds. By applying Proposition
5.3
to (
5.2
), for
\delta\in(0,s-\frac{7}{4})
, the following Strichartz estimate for
d\mathbf{v}_{+}
holds:

\|d\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{4}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H_{x}^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{T}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H_{x}^{s-1}}+\|\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H_{x}^{s-1}}+\|\mathbf{Q}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H_{x}^{s-1}}.

(5.15)

The estimates (
5.15
), (
5.14
) combining with Lemma
3.12
(taking
a=\frac{1}{2}
), Lemma
3.15
, we therefore get

\begin{split}\|d\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{4}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}(1+\|h,\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}})+\|dh,d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{3}(1+\epsilon^{2}_{3})+\epsilon_{3}\epsilon_{2}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(5.16)

By Sobolev imbedding
C^{\delta}_{x}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\hookrightarrow H_{x}^{1+2\delta}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
L^{4}_{t}([-2,2])\hookrightarrow H^{\frac{1}{4}}_{t}([-2,2])
, we then get

\begin{split}\|d\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{4}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}&\lesssim\|d\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}_{t}H_{x}^{1+2\delta}}\\
&\lesssim\|\Lambda^{\frac{1}{4}+2\delta}_{x}d\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}_{t}H_{x}^{\frac{3}{4}}}\\
&\lesssim\|\Lambda^{\frac{1}{4}+2\delta}_{x}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{H^{2}_{t,x}}.\end{split}

(5.17)

By using Lemma
3.7
and
3.11
, we derive that

\begin{split}\|\Lambda^{\frac{1}{4}+2\delta}_{x}\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{H^{2}_{t,x}}\lesssim&\|\Lambda_{x}^{\frac{1}{4}+2\delta}d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}+\|[\Lambda_{x}^{\frac{1}{4}+2\delta},\mathbf{{P}}]v^{\alpha}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{{\frac{1}{4}+2\delta}}}+\|\Lambda_{x}^{\frac{1}{4}+2\delta}\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d^{2}\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{4}+2\delta}}(1+\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}).\end{split}

(5.18)

Substituting (
5.18
) to (
5.17
), and using (
5.14
), it yields

\begin{split}\|d\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{4}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}&\lesssim\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{\frac{5}{4}+2\delta}}(1+\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s}_{x}})\\
&\lesssim\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(5.19)

Adding (
5.16
) and (
5.19
), the following estimate holds:

\begin{split}\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{4}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{4}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{v}_{-}\|_{L^{4}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(5.20)

Since (
5.19
) holds and
\epsilon_{3}\ll\epsilon_{2}
, we can derive the estimate (
5.8
). Therefore, the proof of this proposition is complete.
∎

It remains for us to prove Propositions
5.2
and
5.3
. Since these proofs are lengthy, we will prove Proposition
5.2
in Section
6
, and the proof of Proposition
5.3
will be presented in Section
7
.

6.
Proof of Proposition
5.2

Observing the conclusions in Proposition
5.2
, it depends on the regularity of the null hypersurfaces. We first establish the following result:

Proposition 6.1
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq\frac{15}{8}
. Let
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
so that
\Re(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. Then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\lambda({\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-P_{<\lambda}{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}),dP_{<\lambda}{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta},\lambda^{-1}\nabla P_{<\lambda}d{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

To prove Proposition
6.1
, including Proposition
5.2
, it suffices to restrict our attention to the case where
\theta=(0,1)
and
r=0
. We fix this choice, and suppress
\theta
and
r
in our notation. We use
(x_{2},x^{\prime})
instead of
(x_{\theta},x^{\prime}_{\theta})
. Then
\Sigma
is defined by

\Sigma=\left\{x_{2}-\phi(t,x^{\prime})=0\right\}.

Let
\Delta_{x^{\prime}}
be a Laplacian operator on
\Sigma
, and

\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}=(-\Delta_{x^{\prime}})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(6.1)

The hypothesis
\Re\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
implies that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi_{\theta,r}(t,\cdot)-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\leq 2\epsilon_{1}.

(6.2)

By using (
6.2
) and Sobolev imbedding, we have

\|d\phi(t,x^{\prime})-dt\|_{L^{4}_{t}C^{1,\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}}+\|\partial_{t}d\phi(t,x^{\prime})\|_{L^{4}_{t}C^{\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1},

(6.3)

where we use
s_{0}>\frac{7}{4}
and
\delta_{0}\in(0,s_{0}-\frac{7}{4})
. Noting that
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
, we can obtain

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s,\infty}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s,\infty}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{w}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},\infty}+\|d\mathbf{v},dh,d\mathbf{v}_{+}\|_{L^{4}_{t}C^{\delta}_{x}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(6.4)

We are now ready to prove Proposition
6.1
.

6.1.
Characteristic energy estimates and the proof of Proposition
6.1

To start, let us introduce two lemmas about characteristic energy estimates, which are referred to in
[
61
]
.

Lemma 6.2
.

(
[
61
]
, Lemma 5.5)
Assume
s\in(\frac{7}{4},2]
. Let
\tilde{f}(t,x)=f(t,x^{\prime},x_{2}+\phi(t,x^{\prime}))
. Then we have

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{f}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s-\frac{1}{4},\infty}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s-\frac{1}{4},\infty},\quad\|d\tilde{f}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\lesssim\|df\|_{{L^{4}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}},\quad\|\tilde{f}\|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{s-\frac{1}{4}}_{x}}.

Lemma 6.3
.

(
[
61
]
, Lemma 5.4)
For
r\geq 1
, we have

\begin{split}\sup_{t\in[-2,2]}\|f\|_{H^{r-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2},\\
\sup_{t\in[-2,2]}\|f\|_{H^{r-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma^{t})}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2,\Sigma}.\end{split}

If
r>\frac{3}{2}
, then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{1}f_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2}.

Similarly, if
r>1
, then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{1}f_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2,\Sigma}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2,\Sigma}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2,\Sigma}.

Next, we prove two characteristic energy estimates for the hyperbolic system (
3.1
).

Lemma 6.4
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq\frac{15}{8}
. Suppose that
\mathbf{U}=(p(h),\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{1},\mathrm{e}^{-h}v^{2})^{\mathrm{T}}
satisfies the hyperbolic symmetric system

A^{0}(\mathbf{U})\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}+\sum^{2}_{i=1}A^{i}(\mathbf{U})\partial_{i}\mathbf{U}=0.

(6.5)

Then

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}&\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}.\end{split}

(6.6)

Proof.

By a change of coordinates
x_{2}\rightarrow x_{2}-\phi(t,x^{\prime})
and setting
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}(t,x)=U(t,x^{\prime},x_{2}+\phi(t,x^{\prime}))
, the system (
6.5
) is transformed to

A^{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}A^{i}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{i}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}=-\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{2}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}-\sum_{\alpha=0}^{2}A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}.

For
\phi
is independent of
x_{2}
, we further get

A^{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{t}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}A^{i}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{i}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}=-\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{2}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}-\sum_{\alpha=0}^{2}A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}.

(6.7)

To prove (
6.6
), we first establish the
0
-order estimate. A direct calculation on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
shows that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{0,2,\Sigma}&\lesssim\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|\nabla d\phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x^{\prime}}^{\infty}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|\nabla d\phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

By using Lemma
6.2
, (
6.3
) and (
6.4
), we can prove that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{0,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}.

(6.8)

Next, we will prove the
(s_{0}-\frac{1}{4})
-order estimate. Operating the derivative of
\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}
(
1\leq|\beta|\leq s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}
) on (
6.7
) and integrating it on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we get

\begin{split}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma}}&\lesssim\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}+|I_{1}|+|I_{2}|,\end{split}

(6.9)

where

\begin{split}&I_{1}=-\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\big(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\big)\cdot\Lambda^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau,\\
&I_{2}=-\sum^{2}_{\alpha=0}\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\big(A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\big)\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau.\end{split}

Write
I_{1}
as

\begin{split}I_{1}=&-\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big(\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}(\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}\tilde{\mathbf{U}})-\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\big)\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau\\
&+\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial_{t}\phi\cdot\partial_{3}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau,\\
=&-\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}[\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}},\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}]\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau\end{split}

Similarly,
I_{2}
can be written by

\begin{split}I_{2}=&-\sum^{2}_{\alpha=0}\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big(\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\big(A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\alpha}\tilde{\mathbf{U}})-A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{\alpha}\phi\partial_{\alpha}\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\big)\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau\\
&+\sum^{2}_{\alpha=0}\int_{-2}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big(A^{\alpha}(\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\partial_{\alpha}\phi\big)\cdot\partial_{\alpha}(\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}})\cdot\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}dxd\tau.\end{split}

By using Lemma
3.6
, we infer that

\begin{split}|I_{1}|&\lesssim\big(\|\partial^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\|d\partial_{t}\phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}+\sup_{\theta,r}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\partial_{t}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{\theta,r})}\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\big)\cdot\|\partial^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\end{split}

(6.10)

and

\begin{split}|I_{2}|\lesssim&\big(\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\|d\nabla\phi\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\sup_{\theta,r}\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}d\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{\theta,r})}\big)\cdot\|\partial^{\beta}_{x^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}\\
&+\big(\|d\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}+\|\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}L_{x}^{\infty}}\big)\cdot\|\partial^{\beta}\tilde{\mathbf{U}}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

(6.11)

Take sum of
1\leq\beta\leq s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}
on (
6.9
). By using Lemma
6.2
, (
6.10
), (
6.11
), (
6.4
), and (
6.3
), we obtain

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{x^{\prime}}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}&\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}.\end{split}

(6.12)

Operating
\nabla
on (
6.5
), we have

\begin{split}A^{0}(\mathbf{U})\partial_{t}(\nabla\mathbf{U})+\sum^{2}_{i=1}A^{i}(\mathbf{U})\partial_{i}(\nabla\mathbf{U})=-\sum^{2}_{\alpha=0}\nabla(A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U}))\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{U},\end{split}

In a similar way, we can obtain

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\nabla\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}&\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}.\end{split}

(6.13)

Thanks to
\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}=-\sum^{2}_{i=1}(A^{0})^{-1}A^{i}(\mathbf{U})\partial_{i}\mathbf{U}
and Lemma
6.3
, we can carry out

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}.\end{split}

(6.14)

The estimate (
6.14
) together with (
6.8
), (
6.12
), (
6.13
) gives (
6.6
). This conclude the proof of this lemma.
∎

Lemma 6.5
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq\frac{15}{8}
. Let
\mathbf{U}
satisfy the assumption in Lemma
6.4
. Then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\lambda(\mathbf{U}-P_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U}),dP_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U},\lambda^{-1}d\nabla P_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(6.15)

Proof.

Let
\Delta_{0}
be a standard multiplier of order
0
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, such that
\Delta_{0}
is additionally bounded on
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. Clearly, we have

A^{0}(\mathbf{U})\partial_{t}(\Delta_{0}U)+\sum^{2}_{i=1}A^{i}(\mathbf{U})\partial_{i}(\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U})=-[\Delta_{0},A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})]\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{U}.

Applying Lemma
6.4
, we derive

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{0}U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}+\|[\Delta_{0},A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})]\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}\|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}.

(6.16)

Due to commutator estimates, we obtain

\|[\Delta_{0},A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})]\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}.

According to the inequality (
6.16
), it turns out

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}.

(6.17)

To get a bound for
\lambda(\mathbf{U}-P_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U})
, we write

\lambda(\mathbf{U}-P_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U})=\lambda\sum_{\mu\geq\lambda}P_{\mu}\mathbf{U},

where
P_{\mu}\mathbf{U}
satisfies the above conditions for
\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}
. Applying (
6.17
) and replacing
s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}
to
s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}
, we get

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\lambda(\mathbf{U}-P_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}=&\sum_{\mu\geq\lambda}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mu P_{\mu}\mathbf{U}\cdot\frac{\lambda}{\mu}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{U}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

Taking square of the above inequality, which yields

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\lambda(\mathbf{U}-P_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Finally, applying (
6.16
) to
\Delta_{0}=P_{<\lambda}
and
\Delta_{0}=\lambda^{-1}\nabla P_{<\lambda}
shows that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|dP_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\lambda^{-1}d\nabla P_{<\lambda}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Therefore, the estimate (
6.15
) holds. We have finished the proof of this lemma.
∎

Taking advantage of Lemma
6.4
, inequalities (
5.4
) and (
5.5
), we can directly obtain:

Corollary 6.6
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq\frac{15}{8}
. Suppose
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
. Then the following estimate holds:

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Next, let us prove the characteristic energy estimates for vorticity.

Lemma 6.7
.

Assume
\frac{7}{4}<s_{0}\leq s\leq\frac{15}{8}
. Suppose that
(h,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
. Then we have

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{w}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\mathbf{w}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

(6.18)

Proof.

By a change of coordinates
x_{2}\rightarrow x_{2}-\phi(t,x^{\prime})
and setting
\tilde{\mathbf{w}}(t,x)=\mathbf{w}(t,x^{\prime},x_{2}+\phi(t,x^{\prime}))
, the third equation in (
1.19
) is transformed to

\tilde{v}^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\tilde{w}^{\alpha}=-\tilde{v}^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\phi\tilde{w}^{\alpha}.

For
\phi
is independent of
x_{2}
, we then get

\partial_{t}\tilde{w}^{\alpha}+(\tilde{v}^{0})^{-1}\tilde{v}^{i}\partial_{i}\tilde{w}^{\alpha}=-\partial_{t}\phi\tilde{w}^{\alpha}-(\tilde{v}^{0})^{-1}\tilde{v}^{1}\partial_{1}\phi\tilde{w}^{\alpha}.

(6.19)

Multiplying with
\tilde{w}_{\alpha}
on (
6.19
), and integrating it on
[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we can obtain

\begin{split}\|\tilde{\mathbf{w}}\|^{2}_{0,2,\Sigma}\leq&C\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{w}}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}+C\|d\phi-dt\|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\|\tilde{\mathbf{w}}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

Applying the inequalities (
5.3
) and (
5.5
), it yields

\|\tilde{\mathbf{w}}\|_{0,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(6.20)

For
\mathbf{w}=(w^{0},\mathring{\mathbf{w}})
, we get

\begin{split}\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}=&\|\nabla w^{0}\|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}+\|\nabla{\mathring{\mathbf{w}}}\|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}.\end{split}

By Hodge’s decomposition, we know

\nabla{\mathring{\mathbf{w}}}=(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}\left(\textrm{curl}{\mathring{\mathbf{w}}}+\textrm{div}{\mathring{\mathbf{w}}}\right).

(6.21)

Due to (
3.73
), (
3.74
), and (
6.21
), we can see

\begin{split}\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{W}\|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}+\|(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}\mathbf{W}\|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}.\end{split}

(6.22)

Using (
3.28
), by a change of coordinates, we have

\begin{split}\tilde{v}^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\tilde{W}^{\alpha}=&\tilde{F}^{\alpha}-\tilde{v}^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\phi\tilde{W}^{\alpha},\end{split}

(6.23)

where

\begin{split}F^{\alpha}=&-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w_{\gamma}-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}w_{\gamma}\partial^{\kappa}v_{\kappa}+\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\ \partial_{\beta}w^{\kappa}\partial_{\gamma}v_{\kappa}\\
&+\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\left\{(c^{-2}_{s}-1)v^{\kappa}\right\}\partial_{\kappa}h-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}(c^{-2}_{s}-1)v^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\beta}h\\
&+2\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}c^{-3}_{s}c^{\prime}_{s}v^{\kappa}w_{\gamma}\partial_{\kappa}h\partial_{\beta}h.\end{split}

Operating derivatives
\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\alpha}
(
0\leq\alpha\leq s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}
) on (
6.23
), multiplying with
\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}^{\alpha}\tilde{\mathbf{W}}
, and integrating on
[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we can obtain

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{W}\|^{2}_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\leq&C\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{4}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|{\mathbf{w}}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}+C\|d\phi-dt\|_{s-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\|{\mathbf{w}}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}\\
&+C\|d\phi-dt\|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\|\tilde{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}{\|\nabla{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{8}_{x}}}(1+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}).\end{split}

Taking advantage of inequalities (
5.3
) and (
5.5
), we find that

\|\mathbf{W}\|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(6.24)

Multiplying
(v^{0})^{-1}
on (
3.28
) and operating
(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}
, we have

\begin{split}\partial_{t}(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}W^{\alpha}+(v^{0})^{-1}v^{i}\partial_{i}(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}W^{\alpha}=&(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}\left\{(v^{0})^{-1}F^{\alpha}\right\}\\
&+[(v^{0})^{-1}v^{i}\partial_{i},(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}]W^{\alpha}.\end{split}

(6.25)

The commutator estimate tells us

\|[(v^{0})^{-1}v^{i}\partial_{i},(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}]\mathbf{W}\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{H^{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4}}_{x}}.

(6.26)

Similarly, by change of coordinates on (
6.25
), we can establish

\|(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}\mathbf{W}\|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(6.27)

By trace theorem, we have

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{v})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{4}}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4}}}\|h,\mathbf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(6.28)

Combining (
6.22
), (
6.24
), (
6.27
), and (
6.28
), we get

\begin{split}\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

(6.29)

By using (
6.19
) and (
6.29
), it tells us

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}\|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

(6.30)

Due to
\partial_{x^{\prime}}\mathbf{w}=\nabla\mathbf{w}\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi
, we obtain

\|\tilde{\mathbf{w}}\|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|\nabla\tilde{\mathbf{w}}\|_{s_{0}-\frac{5}{4},2,\Sigma}.

(6.31)

Combining (
6.20
), (
6.29
), (
6.30
) with (
6.31
) yields the estimate (
6.18
). This concludes the proof of this lemma.
∎

Finally, we are ready to prove Proposition
6.1
.

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

From Lemma
6.5
, it only remains for us to verify

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

Due to Lemma
6.4
, (
6.3
), and (
6.4
), we get

\sup_{\theta,r}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}+\sup_{\theta,r}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

By using the definition of
\mathbf{g}
(see (
5.1
)), and Lemma
6.3
, the following estimate

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{v}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|c_{s}^{2}-c_{s}^{2}(0)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{4},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}\\
&\lesssim\epsilon_{2},\end{split}

holds for
s_{0}>\frac{7}{4}
. Therefore, the estimate (
6.1
) holds. This concludes the proof of this lemma.
∎

It also remains for us to obtain Proposition
5.2
. Next, we introduce a new frame on the null hypersurface
\Sigma
.

6.2.
Null frame

We introduce a null frame along
\Sigma
as follows. Let

V=(dr)^{*},

where
r
is the defining function of the foliation
\Sigma
, and where
*
denotes the identification of covectors and vectors induced by
\mathbf{g}
. Then
V
is the null geodesic flow field tangent to
\Sigma
. Let

\sigma=dt(V),\qquad l=\sigma^{-1}V.

Thus
l
is the
\mathbf{g}
-normal field to
\Sigma
normalized so that
dt(l)=1
, hence

l=\left<dt,dx_{2}-d\phi\right>^{-1}_{\mathbf{g}}\left(dx_{2}-d\phi\right)^{*}.

Therefore, the coefficients
l^{j}
are smooth functions of
\mathbf{v},\rho
and
d\phi
. Conversely, we have

dx_{2}-d\phi=\left<l,\partial_{2}\right>^{-1}_{\mathbf{g}}l^{*}.

(6.32)

Seeing from (
6.32
),
d\phi
is also a smooth function of
\mathbf{v},\rho
and the coefficients of
l
.

Next we introduce the vector fields
e_{1}
tangent to the fixed-time slice
\Sigma^{t}
of
\Sigma
. We do this by applying Grahm-Schmidt orthogonalization in the metric
\mathbf{g}
to the
\Sigma^{t}
-tangent vector fields
\partial_{1}+\partial_{1}\phi\partial_{2}
.

Finally, we denote

\underline{l}=l+2\partial_{t}.

It follows that
\{l,\underline{l},e_{1}\}
form a null frame in the sense that

\displaystyle\left<l,\underline{l}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=2,\qquad\qquad\ \ \left<e_{1},e_{1}\right>_{\mathbf{g}}=1,
